We continued our route at southeast by east three quarters of a mile, and landed again to determine the latitude, which is 55° 2′ 51″ To this I add, 2° 45′ southing, which will make the place of taking altitude for time 55° 5′ 36″ with which I find that my timepiece was slow 1:32:23 apparent time; and made the longitude obtained 122° 35′ 50″ west of Greenwich.
From this place we proceeded east by south four miles and a half, east-southeast one mile and a half, in which space there falls in a small river from the east; east half a mile, southeast a mile and a half, east a quarter of a mile, and encamped at seven o’clock. Mr. Mackay and the hunters walked the greatest part of the day, and in the course of their excursion killed a porcupine.14 Here we found the bed of a very large bear quite fresh. During the day several Indian encampments were seen, which were of a late erection. The current had also lost some of its impetuosity during the greater part of the day.
*Saturday, June 8.*﻿—It rained and thundered through the night, and at four in the morning we again encountered the current.

===Chinese===

*——早晨晴朗无风；自我们抵达此地以来，河水已上涨两英寸，水流因而愈发湍急；其流速之猛本已足以令我们对溯流而上感到绝望，若非我们久已习惯于克服这类困难。昨夜我观测了木星第一颗卫星的复出，却一时疏忽，没有把准确时刻写下来就上床睡了：如果我没记错，时计所示为8:18:10。那艘此前简直同残骸差不多的独木舟如今修理完毕，我们向正东行了二英里又四分之一英里，南南东半英里，东南四分之一英里，随后登岸测时。又以东南偏东行四分之三英里，再次登岸测定纬度，为55°2′51″。再加上2°45′的南移，取时处的纬度即为55°5′36″；据此我算出时计比视时慢1:32:23，所得经度为格林尼治以西122°35′50″。
从此地出发，我们向东偏南行了四英里半，东南东一英里半——在这段水路上，有一条小河从东面汇入；又向正东半英里，东南一英里半，正东四分之一英里，七点钟扎营。麦凯先生和猎手们步行了大半天，途中打到一只豪猪。14 我们在这里发现一处十分新鲜的大熊窝。白天还见到几处印第安人的营地，都是新近搭建的。在这一天的绝大部分时间里，水流的凶猛势头也减弱了几分。
*6月8日，星期六*——整夜下雨打雷，凌晨四点我们又迎着水流上路了。

===Original===

Our course was east a quarter of a mile, round to south by east along a very high white sandy bank on the east shore, three quarters of a mile, south-southeast a quarter of a mile, south-southwest a quarter of a mile, south-southeast one mile and a quarter, southeast two miles, with a slack current; southeast by east two miles and a quarter, east a quarter of a mile, south-southeast a quarter of a mile, southeast by south four miles and a half, southeast one mile and a half, south-southwest half a mile, east-northeast half a mile, east-southeast a quarter of a mile, southeast by south one mile, southeast by east half a mile, east by south three quarters of a mile, when the mountains were in full view in this direction, and eastward. For the three last days we could only see them at short intervals and long distances; but till then, they were continually in sight on either side, from our entrance into the fork. Those to the left were at no great distance from us.
For the last two days we had been anxiously looking out for the carrying-place, but could not discover it, and our only hope was in such information as we should be able to procure from the natives. All that remained for us to do, was to push forwards till the river should be no longer navigable: it had now, indeed, overflowed its banks, so that it was eight at night before we could discover a place to encamp. Having found plenty of wild parsnips, we gathered the tops, and boiled them with pemmican for our supper.
*Sunday, June 9.

===Chinese===

我们的航向是：正东四分之一英里，随后沿东岸一道很高的白色沙岸转为南偏东行四分之三英里，南南东四分之一英里，南南西四分之一英里，南南东一英里又四分之一，东南二英里（此段水流舒缓）；东南偏东二英里又四分之一，正东四分之一英里，南南东四分之一英里，东南偏南四英里半，东南一英里半，南南西半英里，东东北半英里，东南东四分之一英里，东南偏南一英里，东南偏东半英里，东偏南四分之三英里——至此，群山在这个方向和东方尽收眼底。此前三天，我们只能隔着长距离偶尔望见它们；但直到那时为止，自进入分岔口起，它们一直连绵不断地出现在我们两侧。左侧的山离我们已不远。
两天来我们一直在焦急地寻找那处搬运点，却始终未能发现，我们唯一的希望便是从土著人那里打听到消息。我们所能做的，只有继续向前推进，直到这条河再不可通航为止：如今河水确实已漫过两岸，以致到了晚上八点我们才找到一处可以宿营的地方。我们发现许多野生欧防风，便采来叶尖，同干肉饼（pemmican）一起煮了当晚饭。
*6月9日，星期日

===Original===

*﻿—The rain of this morning terminated in a heavy mist at half past five, when we embarked and steered southeast one mile and a half, when it veered north-northeast half a mile, southeast three quarters of a mile, east by south three quarters of a mile, east-southeast a quarter of a mile, south-southeast a quarter of a mile, southeast by east one mile, northeast by east half a mile, southeast by east half a mile, southeast by south three quarters of a mile, southeast three quarters of a mile, east by south half a mile, southeast by east half a mile, east-northeast three quarters of a mile, when it veered to south-southeast half a mile, then back to east (when a blue mountain, clear of snow, appeared ahead) one mile and a half; northeast by east half a mile, east by north one mile, when it veered to southeast half a mile, then on to northwest three quarters of a mile, and back to northeast by east half a mile, south by west a quarter of a mile, northeast by east to north-northeast half a mile, south-southeast a quarter of a mile, and east by north half a mile; here we perceived a smell of fire; and in a short time heard people in the woods, as if in a state of great confusion, which was occasioned, as we afterwards understood, by their discovery of us. At the same time this unexpected circumstance produced some little discomposure among ourselves, as our arms were not in a state of preparation, and we were as yet unable to ascertain the number of the party. I considered, that if there were but few, it would be needless to pursue them, as it would not be probable that we should overtake them in these thick woods; and if they were numerous, it would be an act of great imprudence to make the attempt, at least during their present alarm. I therefore ordered my people to strike off to the opposite side, that we might see if any of them had sufficient courage to remain; but, before we were half over the river, which in this part is not more than a hundred yards wide, two men appeared on a rising ground over against us, brandishing their spears, displaying their bows and arrows, and accompanying their hostile gestures with loud vociferations.

===Chinese===

*——今晨的雨到五点半化作浓雾，我们登船出发，朝东南行一英里半，随后河道折向北东北半英里，东南四分之三英里，东偏南四分之三英里，东南东四分之一英里，南南东四分之一英里，东南偏东一英里，东北偏东半英里，东南偏东半英里，东南偏南四分之三英里，东南四分之三英里，东偏南半英里，东南偏东半英里，东东北四分之三英里，随后折向南南东半英里，再转回正东（此时前方出现一座不积雪的青山）一英里半；东北偏东半英里，东偏北一英里，河道又折向东南半英里，接着转向西北四分之三英里，再折回东北偏东半英里，南偏西四分之一英里，由东北偏东转至北东北半英里，南南东四分之一英里，东偏北半英里；在这里我们闻到烟火的气味，不多时又听见林中有人声，仿佛处于极大的慌乱之中——我们后来才明白，那是他们发现了我们的缘故。与此同时，这意外的情况也在我们自己中间引起了一点慌乱，因为我们的武器尚无准备，而且一时还弄不清对方有多少人。我盘算着：倘若对方人少，追赶便无意义，因为在这种密林里多半追不上他们；倘若对方人多，贸然尝试就未免太轻率了，至少在他们眼下惊惶之际是如此。于是我命令我的人向对岸划去，看看他们中间是否有人有胆量留下；但我们还没渡到河心——这里河宽不过一百码——对面高地上便出现了两个人，挥舞着长矛，展示着弓箭，一面做出种种敌意的手势，一面高声叫喊。

===Original===

My interpreter did not hesitate to assure them, that they might dispel their apprehensions, as we were white people, who meditated no injury, but were, on the contrary, desirous of demonstrating every mark of kindness and friendship. They did not, however, seem disposed to confide in our declarations, and actually threatened, if we came over before they were more fully satisfied of our peaceable intentions, that they would discharge their arrows at us. This was a decided kind of conduct which I did not expect; at the same time I readily complied with their proposition, and after some time had passed in hearing and answering their questions, they consented to our landing, though not without betraying very evident symptoms of fear and distrust. They, however, laid aside their weapons, and when I stepped forward and took each of them by the hand, one of them, but with a very tremulous action, drew his knife from his sleeve, and presented it to me as a mark of his submission to my will and pleasure. On our first hearing the noise of these people in the woods, we displayed our flag, which was now shown to them as a token of friendship. They examined us, and everything about us, with a minute and suspicious attention. They had heard, indeed, of white men, but this was the first time that they had ever seen a human being of a complexion different from their own. The party had been here but a few hours; nor had they yet erected their sheds; and, except the two men now with us, they had all fled, leaving their little property behind them. To those which had given us such a proof of their confidence, we paid the most conciliating attentions in our power. One of them I sent to recall his people, and the other, for very obvious reasons, we kept with us. In the meantime the canoe was unloaded, the necessary baggage carried up the hill, and the tents pitched.
Here I determined to remain till the Indians became so familiarized to us, as to give all the intelligence which we imagined might be obtained from them. In fact, it had been my intention to land where I might most probably discover the carrying-place, which was our more immediate object, and undertake marches of two or three days, in different directions, in search of another river.

===Chinese===

我的翻译毫不迟疑地向他们保证不必害怕，说我们是白人，绝无加害之意，相反极愿向他们展示一切友好的情谊。然而他们似乎并不打算相信我们的声明，甚至威胁说，如果在进一步确信我们和平意图之前我们便渡河过来，他们就要向我们放箭。这是一种我没有料到的坚决态度；同时我欣然接受了他们的提议，在来回听问了一些话之后，他们同意我们登岸，尽管仍流露出十分明显的恐惧与猜疑。不过他们放下了武器，当我走上前去同他们一一握手时，其中一人以十分颤抖的动作从袖中抽出刀来，呈递给我，作为他臣服于我意志的表示。刚听到这些人在林中发出声响时，我们便展开了旗帜，此刻又把旗子展示给他们，作为友好的信物。他们以一种细致而多疑的目光打量着我们和我们周围的一切。他们确实听说过白人，但这还是他们头一回亲眼见到肤色与自己不同的人类。这伙人到此处不过几个小时，还没有搭起棚屋；除了眼前这两个人，其余的人都逃走了，把一点点家当丢在了身后。对于这两位给了我们如此信任凭证的人，我们竭尽所能地加以安抚。我派其中一人去召回他的人，另一人则出于非常明显的理由留在我们身边。与此同时，独木舟卸了货，必要的行李搬上了山坡，帐篷也支了起来。
我决定在此地留驻，直到这些印第安人对我们熟悉到肯把我们所设想能从他们那里得到的一切情况都讲出来为止。事实上，我原本的打算，就是在我们最有可能探明搬运点的地方登岸——那是我们更迫近的目标——并向不同方向作两三天的行进，去寻找另一条河。

===Original===

If unsuccessful in this attempt, it was my purpose to continue my progress up the present river, as far as it was navigable, and if we did not meet with natives to instruct us in our further progress, I had determined to return to the fork, and take the other branch, with the hope of better fortune.
It was about three in the afternoon when we landed, and at five the whole party of Indians were assembled. It consisted only of three men, three women, and seven or eight boys and girls. With their scratched legs, bleeding feet, and dishevelled hair, as in the hurry of their flight they had left their shoes and leggins behind them, they displayed a most wretched appearance: they were consoled, however, with beads, and other trifles, which seemed to please them; they had pemmican also given them to eat, which was not unwelcome, and in our opinion, at least, superior to their own provision, which consisted entirely of dried fish.
When I thought that they were sufficiently composed, I sent for the men to my tent, to gain such information respecting the country as I concluded it was in their power to afford me. But my expectations were by no means satisfied: they said that they were not acquainted with any river to the westward, but that there was one from whence they were just arrived, over a carrying-place of eleven days march, which they represented as being a branch only of the river before us. Their ironwork they obtained from the people who inhabit the bank of that river, and an adjacent lake, in exchange for beaver skins, and dressed moose skins. They represented the latter as travelling, during a moon, to get to the country of other tribes, who live in houses, with whom they traffic for the same commodities; and that these also extend their journeys in the same manner to the sea coast, or, to use their expression, the Stinking Lake, where they trade with people like us, that come there in vessels as big as islands. They added, that the people to the westward, as they have been told, are very numerous.

===Chinese===

倘若这一尝试失败，我的计划便是沿眼下的这条河继续上行，到它可通航的尽头为止；如果遇不到为我们指引前程的土著，我便已决定返回分岔口，改走另一条支流，指望运气好些。
我们登岸时约在下午三点，到五点钟，这群印第安人已全部聚齐。总共只有三个男人、三个女人和七八个男孩女孩。他们腿上带着划伤，脚上流着血，头发蓬乱——仓皇出逃时连鞋和绑腿都没顾上穿——一副极其凄惨的样子；不过珠子和其他小玩意安慰了他们，这些似乎很讨他们喜欢；我们还给了他们干肉饼（pemmican）吃，这东西也颇受欢迎，而且至少在我们看来，比他们自己的食物——全是鱼干——要好。
我认为他们已经足够镇定，便把男人们请到我的帐篷里，想从他们口中得到一些情况——我断定他们有能力提供有关这一带地方的信息。但我的期望全然落了空：他们说不知道西边有什么河，只知道有一条河是他们刚从那边过来的，中间要翻越一处需走十一天的搬运点；照他们的说法，那不过是眼前这条河的一条支流。他们的铁器是从住在那条河边和附近一个湖畔的人那里得来的，用海狸皮和鞣好的驼鹿皮交换。据他们讲，后者要走上一整月的路程才能到达其他部族的地界，那些人住在房屋里，他们同这些人交易同样的货物；这些人又以同样的方式把旅程延伸到海边——用他们的话说，是“臭湖”——在那里同像我们一样的人交易，那些人乘着大得像岛屿一样的船来到那里。他们还说，据说西边的人为数众多。

===Original===

Those who inhabit the other branch they stated as consisting of about forty families, while they themselves did not amount to more than a fourth of that number; and were almost continually compelled to remain in their strong holds, where they sometimes perished with cold and hunger, to secure themselves from their enemies, who never failed to attack them whenever an opportunity presented itself.
This account of the country, from a people who I had every reason to suppose were well acquainted with every part of it, threatened to disconcert the project on which my heart was set, and in which my whole mind was occupied. It occurred to me, however, that from fear, or other motives, they might be tardy in their communication; I therefore assured them that, if they would direct me to the river which I described to them, I would come in large vessels, like those that their neighbours had described, to the mouth of it, and bring them arms and ammunition in exchange for the produce of their country; so that they might be able to defend themselves against their enemies, and no longer remain in that abject, distressed, and fugitive state in which they then lived. I added also, that in the meantime, if they would, on my return accompany me below the mountains, to a country which was very abundant in animals, I would furnish them, and their companions, with everything they might want; and make peace between them and the Beaver Indians. But all these promises did not appear to advance the object of my inquiries, and they still persisted in their ignorance of any such river as I had mentioned, that discharged itself into the sea.
In this state of perplexity and disappointment, various projects presented themselves to my mind, which were no sooner formed than they were discovered to be impracticable, and were consequently abandoned. At one time I thought of leaving the canoe, and everything it contained, to go over land, and pursue that chain of connection by which these people obtain their ironwork; but a very brief course of reflection convinced me that it would be impossible for us to carry provisions for our support through any considerable part of such a journey, as well as presents, to secure us a kind reception among the natives, and ammunition for the service of the hunters, and to defend ourselves against any act of hostility.

===Chinese===

据他们陈述，住在另一条支流那边的人约有四十户，而他们自己还不足那个数目的四分之一；他们几乎不得不长年躲在自己的堡垒里，有时因寒冷和饥饿死在里头，以此躲避他们的敌人——那些人一有机会从不放过对他们进攻。
从这样一群我有充分理由相信对这一带每个地方都了如指掌的人口中听到的这番陈述，眼看就要搅乱我倾心投注、全副心神所系的那项计划。然而我转念想到，他们或许是出于恐惧或其他动机，才迟迟不肯吐露实情；于是我向他们保证：只要他们肯为我指引我向他们描述的那条河，我将乘着他们的邻居所描述的那种大船，来到那条河的河口，带去枪支弹药，换取他们地方的物产；这样他们便能自卫御敌，不再过当时那种卑贱、困苦、流离失所的生活。我还补充说，在此期间，如果在我返回时他们肯陪我到群山下游一处动物极多的地方去，我将向他们和他们的同伴提供所需的一切，并在他们与海狸印第安人之间促成和平。但所有这些许诺似乎都无助于推进我探询的目标，他们仍旧一口咬定，不知道有我所说的那样一条流入大海的河。
在这种困惑与失望之中，种种方案接连在我心中浮现，但刚一形成便被发觉行不通，随即被放弃。有一阵子，我想撇下独木舟和舟中所载的一切，取道陆路，沿着这些人获取铁器的那条联系链追踪下去；但略一思索我便确信，要走完这样一段路程中哪怕可观的一小段，我们也不可能既带上维持生计的口粮，又带上确保土著友善接待的礼物，还要带上供猎手使用、并防备任何敌对举动的弹药。

===Original===

At another time my solicitude for the success of the expedition incited a wish to remain with the natives, and go to the sea by the way they had described; but the accomplishment of such a journey, even if no accident should interpose, would have required a portion of time which it was not in my power to bestow. In my present state of information, to proceed further up the river was considered as a fruitless waste of toilsome exertion; and to return unsuccessful, after all our labour, sufferings, and dangers, was an idea too painful to indulge. Besides, I could not yet abandon the hope that the Indians might not yet be sufficiently composed and confident, to disclose their real knowledge of the country freely and fully to me. Nor was I altogether without my doubts respecting the fidelity of my interpreter, who being very much tired of the voyage, might be induced to withhold those communications which would induce me to continue it. I therefore continued my attentions to the natives, regaled them with such provisions as I had, indulged their children with a taste of sugar, and determined to suspend my conversation with them till the following morning. On my expressing a desire to partake of their fish, they brought me a few dried trout, well cured, that had been taken in the river which they lately left. One of the men also brought me five beaver skins, as a present.
*Monday, June 10.*﻿—The solicitude that possessed my mind interrupted my repose; when the dawn appeared I had already quitted my bed, and was waiting with impatience for another conference with the natives. The sun, however, had risen before they left their leafy bowers, whither they had retired with their children, having most hospitably resigned their beds, and the partners of them, to the solicitations of my young men.
I now repeated my inquiries, but my perplexity was not removed by any favourable variation in their answers. About nine, however, one of them, still remaining at my fire, in conversation with the interpreters, I understood enough of his language to know that he mentioned something about a great river, at the same time pointing significantly up that which was before us.

===Chinese===

又有一些时候，对远征成败的忧切使我恨不得留在土著中间，循他们描述的那条路去海边；但这样一段旅程，即使不出任何意外，所需的时间也远非我所能付出。以我眼下掌握的情况而论，继续溯河而上只会是白白耗费辛苦；而历经这一切劳苦、艰辛与危险之后空手而归，这念头又痛苦得令人不敢多想。何况我还不肯放弃一线希望：这些印第安人也许尚未镇定和放心到足以把他们真正的地理知识毫无保留、痛痛快快地告诉我。我对翻译的忠实也并非全无怀疑，他已十分厌倦这次航行，也许会存心扣下那些足以促使我继续前进的情报。因此我继续对土著殷勤相待，用我现有的食物款待他们，任由他们的孩子尝一尝糖的滋味，并决定把与他们的谈话推迟到第二天早晨。当我表示想尝尝他们的鱼时，他们给我送来几条腌得很好的干鳟鱼，是他们不久前离开的那条河里捕的。其中一个男人还送来五张海狸皮作为礼物。
*6月10日，星期一*——心中的焦虑搅扰了我的睡眠；天刚破晓我已起身，急不可耐地等着同土著再谈一次。然而太阳升起好一阵之后，他们才离开那些绿叶棚——头天晚上他们把自己的床铺，连同床上的伴侣，极其好客地让给了我的年轻人们，自己带着孩子退到那里过夜。
我再次重复询问，但他们的回答没有任何有利于我的变化，我的困惑依旧不得解脱。不过九点钟左右，其中一个仍留在我的火堆旁同翻译们交谈的人，说了一句我多少听得懂的话——他提到了一条大河，同时意味深长地指着我们面前这条河的上方。

===Original===

On my inquiring of the interpreter respecting that expression, I was informed that he knew of a large river, that runs towards the midday sun, a branch of which flowed near the source of that which we were now navigating; and that there were only three small lakes, and as many carrying-places, leading to a small river, which discharges itself into the great river, but that the latter did not empty itself into the sea. The inhabitants, he said, built houses, lived on islands, and were a numerous and warlike people. I desired him to describe the road to the other river, by delineating it with a piece of coal, on a strip of bark, which he accomplished to my satisfaction. The opinion that the river did not discharge itself into the sea, I very confidently imputed to his ignorance of the country.
My hopes were now renewed, and an object presented itself which awakened my utmost impatience. To facilitate its attainment, one of the Indians was induced, by presents, to accompany me as a guide to the first inhabitants, which we might expect to meet on the small lakes in our way. I accordingly resolved to depart with all expedition, and while my people were making every necessary preparation, I employed myself in writing the following description of the natives around me:
They are low in stature, not exceeding five feet six or seven inches; and they are of that meagre appearance which might be expected in a people whose life is one secession of difficulties, in procuring subsistence. Their faces are round, with high cheek bones; and their eyes, which are small, are of a dark brown colour; the cartilage of their nose is perforated, but without any ornaments suspended from it; their hair is of a dingy black, hanging loose and in disorder over their shoulders, but irregularly cut in the front, so as not to obstruct the sight; their beards are eradicated, with the exception of a few straggling hairs, and their complexion is a swarthy yellow.
Their dress consists of robes made of the skins of the heaver, the groundhog and the reindeer, dressed in the hair, and of the moose-skin without it. All of them are ornamented with a fringe, while some of them have tassels hanging down the seams; those of the groundhog are decorated on the fur side with the tails of the animal, which they do not separate from them.

===Chinese===

我就那句话向翻译询问，得到的答复是：他知道有一条大河朝着正午太阳的方向流去，它的一条支流流经我们此刻航行的这条河的源头附近；到那条大河，只须经过三个小湖和同样数目的搬运点，便有一条小河注入那条大河；但那条大河并不入海。据他说，大河沿岸的居民盖房屋、住在岛上，是一个人口众多、骁勇好战的民族。我让他把通往另一条河的路线用一块炭在一条树皮上画出来，他画得令我满意。至于那条河不入海的说法，我很有把握地把它归因于他对这一带的无知。
我的希望重新燃起，一个目标出现在眼前，唤起我最大的急切。为了便于达到这个目标，我用礼物劝动一名印第安人做向导，陪我去往途中那些小湖上可能遇到的第一批居民那里。于是我决定尽快启程，趁部下们做各项必要准备的时候，我动手写下身边这些土著的如下描述：
他们身材矮小，不超过五英尺六七英寸；一生都在接连不断地为觅食操劳，可想而知他们形容瘦削。脸圆而颧骨高；眼睛细小，呈深褐色；鼻软骨上穿了孔，却不悬挂任何饰物；头发呈暗黑色，蓬乱地披散在肩上，前面参差地剪过，以免遮住视线；胡须拔得精光，只余几茎稀疏的毫毛，肤色为黝黄色。
他们的衣着由带毛鞣制的海狸皮、旱獭皮和驯鹿皮袍子，以及不带毛的驼鹿皮袍子组成。所有袍子都镶有流苏边，有些沿着接缝垂着缨穗；旱獭皮袍在毛面一侧缀着这种动物的尾巴，他们不把尾巴从皮子上割下来。

===Original===

Their garments they tie over the shoulders, and fasten them round the middle with a belt of green skin, which is as stiff as horn. Their leggins are long, and, if they were topped with a waistband, might be called trousers: they, as well as their shoes, are made of dressed moose, elk, or reindeer skin. The organs of generation they leave uncovered.
The women differ little in their dress, from the men, except in the addition of an apron, which is fastened round the waist, and hangs down to the knees. They are in general of a more lusty make than the other sex, and taller in proportion, but infinitely their inferiors in cleanliness. A black artificial stripe crosses the face beneath the eye, from ear to ear, which I first took for scabs, from the accumulation of dirt on it. Their hair, which is longer than that of the men, is divided from the forehead to the crown, and drawn back in long plaits behind the ears. They have also a few white beads, which they get where they procure their iron: they are from a line to an inch in length, and are worn in their ears, but are not of European manufacture. These, with bracelets made of horn and bone, compose all the ornaments which decorate their persons. Necklaces of the grizzly or white bear’s claws, are worn exclusively by the men.
Their arms consist of bows made of cedar, six feet in length, with a short iron spike at one end, and serve occasionally as a spear. Their arrows are well made, barbed, and pointed with iron, flint, stone, or bone; they are feathered, and from two or two feet and a half in length. They have two kinds of spears, but both are double edged, and of well polished iron; one of them is about twelve inches long, and two wide; the other about half the width, and two thirds of the length; the shafts of the first are eight feet in length, and the latter six. They have also spears made of bone. Their knives consist of pieces of iron, shaped and handled by themselves. Their axes are something like our adze, and they use them in the same manner as we employ that instrument.

===Chinese===

他们的衣袍在双肩系住，腰间用一条绿皮腰带束紧，那皮带硬得如同角质。绑腿很长，若在顶端加上一条腰带，便可称作裤子：绑腿和鞋都用鞣制的驼鹿皮、麋鹿皮或驯鹿皮做成。生殖器官则任其裸露。
女人的衣着与男人差别不大，只是多一条围裙，系在腰间，垂到膝盖。她们一般比异性更结实，身材比例上也更高，但在清洁方面却远逊于男人。一道黑色的人为条纹从一只耳到另一只耳横过眼下方的面部，起初我还以为是积垢结的痂。她们的头发比男人长，从前额一直分到头顶，在耳后拢成长辫。她们还有几颗白珠子，是从弄到铁器的同一地方得来的：长自一英寸不等，戴在耳朵上，但并非欧洲制造。这些，加上角质和骨质的镯子，便是装点她们周身的全部饰物。灰熊或白熊的爪子做成的项链，则为男人所专用。
他们的武器包括雪松木做的弓，长六英尺，一端装有短铁尖，必要时可当长矛使用。箭制作精良，有倒钩，箭镞为铁、燧石、石头或骨；箭尾有羽，长两英尺至两英尺半。他们有两种矛，都是双刃，铁质磨得很好：一种约十二英寸长、两英寸宽；另一种宽约其半，长约其三分之二；前者的杆长八英尺，后者六英尺。他们还有骨制的矛。刀是用铁片做的，形状和柄都是自己打制的。斧头有点像我们的锛子，用法也同我们使用那种工具一样。

===Original===

They were, indeed, furnished with iron in a manner that I could not have supposed, and plainly proved to me that their communication with those, who communicate with the inhabitants of the sea coast, cannot be very difficult, and from their ample provision of iron weapons, the means of procuring it must be of a more distant origin than I had at first conjectured.
They have snares made of green skin, which they cut to the size of sturgeon twine, and twist a certain number of them together; and though when completed they do not exceed the thickness of a cod-line, their strength is sufficient to hold a moose-deer; they are from one and a half to two fathoms in length. Their nets and fishing-lines are made of willow-bark and nettles; those made of the latter are finer and smoother than if made with hempen thread. Their hooks are small bones, fixed in pieces of wood split for that purpose, and tied round with fine watape, which has been particularly described in the former voyage. Their kettles are also made of watape, which is so closely woven that they never leak, and they heat water in them, by putting red-hot stones into it. There is one kind of them, made of spruce-bark, which they hang over the fire, but at such a distance as to receive the heat without being within reach of the blaze; a very tedious operation. They have various dishes of wood and bark; spoons of horn and wood, and buckets; bags of leather and network, and baskets of bark, some of which hold their fishing-tackle, while others are contrived to be carried on the back. They have a brown kind of earth in great abundance, with which they rub their clothes, not only for ornament but utility, as it prevents the leather from becoming hard after it has been wetted. They have spruce bark in great plenty, with which they make their canoes, an operation that does not require any great portion of skill or ingenuity, and is managed in the following manner.

===Chinese===

他们拥有铁器的充足程度，的确出乎我的意料，这清楚地向 我表明，他们与那些同海岸居民往来的人之间的交通必定不难；而从他们铁制武器的丰富储备来看，铁的来源必定比我起初设想的更为遥远。
他们有绿皮做的陷阱索，把皮子切成鲟鱼线粗细，再把若干股捻在一起；成品虽不超过一根鳕鱼线的粗细，其强度却足以缚住一头驼鹿；长约一英寻半至二英寻。他们的网和钓线用柳树皮和荨麻制成；荨麻做的比麻线做的还要细密光滑。鱼钩是小骨，夹在为此劈开的木片里，用细韧皮绳（watape）缠牢——这种韧皮绳在前一次航行中已有专门描述。他们的水壶也用韧皮绳编成，编织得滴水不漏，把烧红的石头放进去烧水。还有一种用云杉皮做的，吊在火上，但隔着相当的距离，只受热气而不近火焰；那是一道极其磨人的工序。他们有各种木制和树皮做的盘碟，角质和木质的勺子，还有提桶；皮革和网线做的袋子，树皮编的篮子，有些装渔具，有些则做成可以背在背上的式样。他们有一种储量极丰的棕色泥土，用来擦拭衣服，既为美观也为实用，能防止皮子浸水后变硬。他们还有大量的云杉皮，用来造独木舟，这道工序并不需要多少技艺或巧思，做法如下。

===Original===

﻿—The bark is taken off the tree the whole length of the intended canoe, which is commonly about eighteen feet, and is sewed with watape at both ends; two laths are then laid, and fixed along the edge of the bark which forms the gunwale; in these are fixed the bars, and against them bear the ribs or timbers, that are out to the length to which the bark can be stretched; and, to give additional strength, strips of wood are laid between them: to make the whole watertight, gum is abundantly employed. These vessels carry from two to five people. Canoes of a similar construction were used by the Beaver Indians within these few years, but they now very generally employ those made of the bark of the birch tree, which are by far more durable. Their paddles are about six feet long, and about one foot is occupied by the blade, which is in the shape of an heart.
Previous to our departure, the natives had caught a couple of trout, of about six pounds weight, which they brought me, and I paid them with beads. They likewise gave me a net, made of nettles, the skin of a moose-deer, dressed, and a white horn in the shape of a spoon which resembles the horn of the buffalo of the Coppermine River; but their description of the animal to which it belongs does not answer to that. My young men also got two quivers of excellent arrows, a collar of white bear’s claws, of a great length, horn bracelets, and other articles, for which they received an ample remuneration.

===Chinese===

——按照预备打造的独木舟的长度，把整张树皮从树上剥下来，一般约十八英尺，两端用韧皮绳缝拢；随后沿构成舷缘的皮边放上并固定两根板条；板条上安横档，紧抵横档的是肋骨或船骨，长度截到树皮所能绷展的限度；为增加强度，肋骨之间还垫上木条；为使整体不漏水，还大量使用树胶。这种船可乘两到五人。直到几年之前，海狸印第安人（Beaver Indians）还在使用同样构造的独木舟，但如今他们已普遍改用桦树皮造的，那种要耐用得多。他们的桨约六英尺长，桨叶约占一英尺，呈心形。
临行之前，土著人捕到两条约六磅重的鳟鱼，给我送来，我用珠子付了账。他们还送了我一张荨麻编的网、一张鞣好的驼鹿皮，还有一支白角质勺子，形状与铜矿河（Coppermine River）野牛的角相似；但他们对这种角所属动物的描述却与之对不上。我的年轻人们也得到两筒上好的箭、一条很长的白熊爪项圈、角质镯子和其他物件，土著们为此收到了丰厚的酬报。

### VI / 六
===Original===

*Monday, June 10.*﻿—At ten we were ready to embark. I then took leave of the Indians, but encouraged them to expect us in two moons, and expressed an hope that I should find them on the road with any of their relations whom they might meet. I also returned the beaver skins to the man who had presented them to me, desiring him to take care of them till I came back, when I would purchase them of him. Our guide expressed much less concern about the undertaking in which he had engaged, than his companions, who appeared to be affected with great solicitude for his safety.
We now pushed off the canoe from the bank, and proceeded east half a mile, when a river flowed in from the left, about half as large as that which we were navigating. We continued the same course three quarters of a mile, when we missed two of our fowling pieces, which had been forgotten, and I sent their owners back for them, who were absent on this errand upwards of an hour. We now proceeded northeast by east half a mile, northeast by north three quarters of a mile, when the current slackened; there was a verdant spot on the left, where, from the remains of some Indian timber-work, it appeared, that the natives have frequently encamped. Our next course was east one mile, and we saw a ridge of mountains covered with snow to the southeast. The land on our right was low and marshy for three or four miles, when it rose into a range of heights that extended to the mountains. We proceeded east-southeast a mile and a half, southeast by east one mile, east by south three quarters of a mile, southeast by east one mile, east by south half a mile, northeast by east one mile, southeast half a mile, east-northeast a mile and a quarter, south-southeast half a mile, north-northeast a mile and a half: here a river flowed in from the left, which was about one-fourth part as large as that which received its tributary waters. We then continued east by south half a mile, to the foot of the mountain on the south of the above river.

===Chinese===

*6月10日，星期一*——十点，我们做好了出发的准备。我随即向印第安人辞行，但鼓励他们等着我们在两个月亮内回来，并表示希望届时能在路上见到他们，同他们可能遇到的任何亲属在一起。我还把海狸皮还给赠我皮子的那个人，请他代为保管，等我回来再向他购买。我们的向导对自己所承担的这趟差事所表现的担忧，远比他的同伴们少——同伴们似乎对他的安全极为挂念。
我们把独木舟推离河岸，向正东行了半英里，一条河从左边汇入，约为我们正航行的这条河的一半大。我们继续同一航向行四分之三英里，这时发现少了两支猎枪——先前被遗忘了，我派枪的主人回去取，他们为这桩差事离开了一个多小时。随后我们向东北偏东行半英里，东北偏北四分之三英里，水流渐缓；左边有一片青翠的地方，从一些印第安木构工程的残迹来看，土著们常在此扎营。接下来我们向正东行一英里，望见东南方向有一道积雪的山脉。右侧的土地有三四英里低洼多沼，随后隆起为一道高地，绵延至群山。我们又向东南东行一英里半，东南偏东一英里，东偏南四分之三英里，东南偏东一英里，东偏南半英里，东北偏东一英里，东南半英里，东东北一英里又四分之一，南南东半英里，北东北一英里半：这里又有一条河从左边汇入，约为接纳它这条河的四分之一大。随后我们继续东偏南行半英里，来到上述那条河以南的山脚下。

===Original===

The course now veered short, southwest by west three quarters of a mile, east by south a quarter of a mile, south half a mile, southeast by south half a mile, southwest a quarter of a mile, east by south a quarter of a mile, veered to west-northwest a quarter of a mile, southwest one eighth of a mile, east-southeast one quarter of a mile, east one sixth of a mile, south-southwest one twelfth of a mile, east-southeast one eighth of a mile, northeast by east one third of a mile, east by north one twelfth of a mile, northeast by east one third of a mile, east one sixteenth of a mile, southeast one twelfth of a mile, northeast by east one twelfth of a mile, east one eighth of a mile, and east-southeast half a mile, when we landed at seven o’clock and encamped. During the greatest part of the distance we came today, the river runs close under the mountains on the left.
*Tuesday, June 11.*﻿—The morning was clear and cold. On my interpreter’s encouraging the guide to dispel all apprehension, to maintain his fidelity to me, and not to desert in the night, “How is it possible for me,” he replied, “to leave the lodge of the Great Spirit!﻿—When he tells me that he has no further occasion for me, I will then return to my children.” As we proceeded, however, he soon lost, and with good reason, his exalted notions of me.
At four we continued our voyage, steering east by south a mile and a half, east-southeast half a mile. A river appeared on the left, at the foot of a mountain which, from its conical form, my young Indian called the Beaver Lodge Mountain. Having proceeded south-southeast half a mile, another river appeared from the right. We now came in a line with the beginning of the mountains we saw yesterday: others of the same kind ran parallel with them on the left side of the river, which was reduced to the breadth of fifteen yards, and with a moderate current.

===Chinese===

河道此刻急转，西南偏西四分之三英里，东偏南四分之一英里，正南半英里，东南偏南半英里，西南四分之一英里，东偏南四分之一英里，折向西西北四分之一英里，西南八分之一英里，东南东四分之一英里，正东六分之一英里，南南西十二分之一英里，东南东八分之一英里，东北偏东三分之一英里，东偏北十二分之一英里，东北偏东三分之一英里，正东十六分之一英里，东南十二分之一英里，东北偏东十二分之一英里，正东八分之一英里，又东南东半英里，七点钟我们登岸扎营。今日所行路程的大部分，河道都紧贴着左侧的群山。
*6月11日，星期二*——早晨晴朗而寒冷。我的翻译鼓励向导打消一切顾虑，对我保持忠心，不要在夜里溜走，他答道：“我怎么可能离开‘大神’的居所呢！——等他对我说不再用得着我的时候，我自会回到我的孩子们身边去。”然而随着行程推进，他很快就——而且不无理由地——失去了对我的那副崇敬看法。
四点，我们继续航行，朝东偏南行一英里半，东南东半英里。左边一条河出现在一座山的山脚下；我的年轻印第安人因其圆锥形的山体，把那山叫作海狸居山。向正南南东行过半英里后，右边又出现一条河。此刻我们与昨日望见的那道山脉的起点连成一线：河的左侧另有同样的一道山与它平行，河面收窄到十五码，水流平缓。

===Original===

We now steered east-northeast one eighth of a mile, southeast by south one eighth of a mile, east-southeast one sixth of a mile, southwest one eighth of a mile, east-southeast one eighth of a mile, south-southeast one sixth of a mile, northeast by east one twelfth of a mile, east-southeast half a mile, southwest by west one third of a mile, south-southeast one eighth of a mile, south-southwest one quarter of a mile, northeast one sixth of a mile, south by west one fourth of a mile, east three quarters of a mile, and northeast one quarter of a mile. Here the mountain on the left appeared to be composed of a succession of round hills, covered with wood almost to their summits, which were white with snow, and crowned with withered trees. We now steered east, in a line with the high lands on the right five miles; north one twelfth of a mile, northeast by north one eighth of a mile, south by east one sixteenth of a mile, northeast by north one fourth of a mile, where another river fell in from the right; northeast by east one sixth of a mile, east two miles and a half, south one twelfth of a mile, northeast half a mile, southeast one third of a mile, east one mile and a quarter, south-southwest one sixteenth of a mile, northeast by east half a mile, east one mile and three quarters, south and southwest by west half a mile, northeast half a mile, south one third of a mile, northeast by north one sixth of a mile, east by south one fourth of a mile, south one eighth of a mile, southeast three quarters of a mile.

===Chinese===

我们随即向东东北行八分之一英里，东南偏南八分之一英里，东南东六分之一英里，西南八分之一英里，东南东八分之一英里，南南东六分之一英里，东北偏东十二分之一英里，东南东半英里，西南偏西三分之一英里，南南东八分之一英里，南南西四分之一英里，东北六分之一英里，南偏西四分之一英里，正东四分之三英里，再东北四分之一英里。这里，左侧的山由一连串浑圆的丘陵组成，林木几乎覆盖到山顶，山顶覆着白雪，簇立着枯树。我们随即向正东行进，与右侧的高地取直五英里；向北十二分之一英里，东北偏北八分之一英里，南偏东十六分之一英里，东北偏北四分之一英里，又有一条河从右边汇入；东北偏东六分之一英里，正东二英里半，向南十二分之一英里，东北半英里，东南三分之一英里，正东一英里又四分之一，南南西十六分之一英里，东北偏东半英里，正东一英里又四分之三，向南及西南偏西半英里，东北半英里，向南三分之一英里，东北偏北六分之一英里，东偏南四分之一英里，向南八分之一英里，东南四分之三英里。

===Original===

The canoe had taken in so much water, that it was necessary for us to land here, in order to stop the leakage, which occasioned the delay of an hour and a quarter, northeast a quarter of a mile, east-northeast a quarter of a mile, southeast by south a sixteenth of a mile, east by south a twelfth of a mile, northeast one sixth of a mile, east-southeast one sixteenth of a mile, southwest half a mile, northeast a quarter of a mile, east by south half a mile, south-southeast one twelfth of a mile, east half a mile, northeast by north a quarter of a mile, south-southeast a quarter of a mile, northeast by north one twelfth of a mile, where a small river flowed in from the left, southeast by east one twelfth of a mile, south by east a quarter of a mile, southeast one eighth of a mile, east one twelfth of a mile, northeast by north a quarter of a mile, south half a mile, southeast by south one eighth of a mile, northeast one fourth of a mile, southeast by east, and southeast by south one third of a mile, east-southeast, and north-northeast one third of a mile, and south by west, east and east-northeast one eighth of a mile.
Here we quitted the main branch, which, according to the information of our guide, terminates at a short distance, where it is supplied by the snow which covers the mountains. In the same direction is a valley which appears to be of very great depth, and is full of snow, that rises nearly to the height of the land, and forms a reservoir of itself sufficient to furnish a river, whenever there is a moderate degree of heat. The branch which we left was not, at this time, more than ten yards broad, while that which we entered was still less. Here the current was very trifling, and the channel so meandering, that we sometimes found it difficult to work the canoe forward. The straight course from this to the entrance of a small lake or pond, is about east one mile. This entrance by the river into the lake was almost choked up by a quantity of driftwood, which appeared to me to be an extraordinary circumstance: but I afterwards found that it falls down from the mountains. The water, however, was so high, that the country was entirely overflowed, and we passed with the canoe among the branches of trees.

===Chinese===

独木舟已经进了太多水，我们必须在此登岸堵漏，为此耽搁了一个小时一刻钟，随后向东北行四分之一英里，东东北四分之一英里，东南偏南十六分之一英里，东偏南十二分之一英里，东北六分之一英里，东南东十六分之一英里，西南半英里，东北四分之一英里，东偏南半英里，南南东十二分之一英里，正东半英里，东北偏北四分之一英里，南南东四分之一英里，东北偏北十二分之一英里，一条小河从左边流进来；再东南偏东十二分之一英里，南偏东四分之一英里，东南八分之一英里，正东十二分之一英里，东北偏北四分之一英里，正南半英里，东南偏南八分之一英里，东北四分之一英里，东南偏东与东南偏南各三分之一英里，东南东与北东北各三分之一英里，再南偏西、正东与东东北各八分之一英里。
在这里，我们离开了主河道——据向导说，它在前面不远处便到了尽头，靠覆盖山上的积雪补给。同一方向上有一道山谷，看起来极深，谷中积雪几乎高与地齐，本身便自成一座水库，只要有一定程度的热量，便足以供应出一条河来。我们离开的那条支流此时宽不过十码，而我们驶入的这条更窄。这里水流极其微弱，河道又蜿蜒曲折，有时我们简直难以让独木舟前进。从这里到一个小湖或池塘的入口，直线航向约为正东一英里。河水入湖的河口几乎被大量浮木堵死，这在我看来是桩怪事：但我后来才知道，这些木头是从山上塌落下来的。然而水势极高，四野尽成泽国，我们是驾着独木舟从树木的枝桠间穿过去的。

===Original===

The principal wood along the banks is spruce, intermixed with a few white birch, growing on detached spots, the intervening spaces being covered with willow and elder. We advanced about a mile in the lake, and took up our station for the night at an old Indian encampment. Here we expected to meet with natives, but were disappointed; but our guide encouraged us with the hope of seeing some on the morrow. We saw beaver in the course of the afternoon, but did not discharge our pieces from the fear of alarming the inhabitants; there were also swans in great numbers, with geese and ducks, which we did not disturb for the same reason. We observed also the tracks of moose-deer that had crossed the river; and wild parsnips grew here in abundance, which have been already mentioned as a grateful vegetable. Of birds, we saw bluejays, yellow birds, and one beautiful hummingbird; of the first and last, I had not seen any since I had been in the northwest.
*Wednesday, June 12.*﻿—The weather was the same as yesterday, and we proceeded between three and four in the morning. We took up the net which we had set the preceding evening, when it contained a trout, one white fish, one carp, and three jub. The lake is about two miles in length, east by south, and from three to five hundred yards wide. This I consider as the highest and southernmost source of the Unjigah, or Peace River, latitude, 54° 24′ north, longitude 121° west from Greenwich, which, after a winding course through a vast extent of country, receiving many large rivers in its progress, and passing through the Slave Lake, empties itself into the Frozen Ocean, in 70° north latitude, and about 135° west longitude.
We landed and unloaded, where we found a beaten path leading over a low ridge of land eight hundred and seventeen paces in length, to another small lake. The distance between the two mountains at this place is about a quarter of a mile, rocky precipices presenting themselves on both sides. A few large spruce trees and liards were scattered over the carrying-place. There were also willows along the side of the water, with plenty of grass and weeds. The natives had left their old canoes here, with baskets hanging on the trees, which contained various articles. From the latter I took a net, some hooks, a goat’s-horn, and a kind of wooden trap, in which, as our guide informed me, the groundhog is taken.

===Chinese===

沿岸主要的树木是云杉，夹杂着几株白桦，长在一块块零散的地方，间隔的地面上覆着柳树和接骨木。我们在湖中行进了约一英里，在一处旧印第安营地落脚过夜。我们本指望在这里遇到土著，却落了空；但向导鼓起我们的希望，说明天便能见到一些。下午我们看见了海狸，却因怕惊动居民而没有开枪；还有大量的天鹅，以及雁和鸭，出于同样的原因我们未去惊扰它们。我们还观察到渡河的驼鹿踪迹；野生欧防风在这里长得极茂盛——前文已提到，那是一种可口的蔬菜。鸟类中，我们见到蓝松鸦、黄鸟，还有一只美丽的蜂鸟；头一种和最后一种，是我到西北以来从未见过的。
*6月12日，星期三*——天气同昨天一样，凌晨三四点之间我们上路了。我们收起前一晚下的网，网里有一条鳟鱼、一条白鱼、一条鲤和三条白鲑。此湖长约二英里，走向东偏南，宽三百至五百码。我认为这里便是温吉加（Unjigah），即和平河（Peace River）最高、最南的源头，北纬54°24′，格林尼治以西西经121°；这条河经过蜿蜒的流程穿越一片广袤的地域，沿途接纳许多大河，流经奴隶湖，最终在北纬70°、西经约135°处注入冰洋（Frozen Ocean）。
我们登岸卸货，发现一条踩出的小路，翻过一道低矮的山梁，长八百一十七步，通往另一个小湖。此处两山相距约四分之一英里，两侧都是岩石峭壁。搬运点上散布着几株大云杉和白杨。水边还长着柳树，青草和水草都很丰茂。土著们把旧独木舟弃置在这里，树上挂着篮子，里面装着各色物件。我从篮子里取了一张网、几枚鱼钩、一支山羊角，还有一种木制的捕捉器——据向导相告，是用来捉旱獭的。

===Original===

I left, however, in exchange, a knife, some fire-steels, beads, awls, etc. Here two streams tumble down the rocks from the right, and lose themselves in the lake which we had left; while two others fall from the opposite heights, and glide into the lake which we were approaching; this being the highest point of land dividing these waters, and we are now going with the stream. This lake runs in the same course as the last, but is rather narrower, and not more than half the length. We were obliged to clear away some floating driftwood to get to the carrying-place, over which is a beaten path of only an hundred and seventy-five paces long. The lake empties itself by a small river, which, if the channel were not interrupted by large trees that had fallen across it, would have admitted of our canoe with all its lading: the impediment, indeed, might have been removed by two axe-men in a few hours. On the edge of the water, we observed a large quantity of thick yellow, scum or froth, of an acrid taste and smell.
We embarked on this lake, which is in the same course, and about the same size as that which we had just left, and from whence we passed into a small river, that was so full of fallen wood, as to employ some time, and require some exertion, to force a passage. At the entrance, it afforded no more water than was just sufficient to bear the canoe; but it was soon increased by many small streams which came in broken rills down the rugged sides of the mountains, and were furnished, as I suppose, by the melting of the snow. These accessory streamlets had all the coldness of ice. Our course continued to be obstructed by banks of gravel, as well as trees which had fallen across the river. We were obliged to force our way through the one, and to cut through the other, at a great expense of time and trouble. In many places the current was also very rapid and meandering. At four in the afternoon, we stopped to unload and carry, and at five we entered a small round lake of about one third of a mile in diameter. From the last lake to this is, I think, in a straight line, east by south six miles, though it is twice that distance by the winding of the river. We again entered the river, which soon ran with great rapidity, and rushed impetuously over a bed of flat stones.

===Chinese===

不过我留下一把刀、几块火钢、珠子、锥子等物作为交换。这里有两股溪水从右侧滚落岩石，消失在我们刚离开的湖里；另有两条从对面的高处跌下，滑入我们将要前往的湖中；这里是分水岭的最高点，从此我们就要顺流而下了。这个湖与上一个走向相同，但略窄，长度不过其半。我们不得不清开一些漂浮的浮木才能到达搬运点，上面的踩出小路只有一百七十五步长。湖水经由一条小河排出，若不是河道被横倒的大树阻断，本可容我们的独木舟连货载一起通过：其实那障碍，两名斧手花几个小时便可清除。在水边，我们观察到一大片浓黄的泡沫或浮渣，气味和味道都带刺激性。
我们在这个湖上登船，它与刚离开的那个走向相同、大小相仿，随后从那里进入一条小河，河中倒木密布，费了不少时间和气力才强行开出一条通道。入口处的水刚够托起独木舟；但很快便有许多小溪从山的两壁蜴蜴处碎流而下，水量大增——我猜想那是积雪消融所补给的。这些支流的小水全都冰冷如冰。我们的航路不断被砾石滩和横倒河上的树木所阻。我们不得不从前者中硬闯，把后者砍开，耗费了大量的时间和劳力。许多地方水流既急又曲折。下午四点，我们停下卸货搬运，五点进入一个直径约三分之一英里的小圆湖。从上一个湖到这里，我想直线为东偏南六英里，尽管顺河的弯道要走两倍的路程。我们重新驶入河道，河水很快便奔流湍急，在一片扁平石头的河床上汹涌冲过。

===Original===

At half past six we were stopped by two large trees that lay across the river, and it was with great difficulty that the canoe was prevented from driving against them. Here we unloaded and formed our encampment.
The weather was cloudy and raw, and as the circumstances of this day’s voyage had compelled us to be frequently in the water, which was cold as ice, we were almost in a benumbed state. Some of the people who had gone ashore to lighten the canoe, experienced great difficulty in reaching us, from the rugged state of the country; it was, indeed, almost dark when they arrived. We had no sooner landed than I sent two men down the river to bring me some account of its circumstances, that I might form a judgment of the difficulties which might await us on the morrow; and they brought back a fearful detail of rapid currents, fallen trees, and large stones. At this place our guide manifested evident symptoms of discontent: he had been very much alarmed in going down some of the rapids with us, and expressed an anxiety to return. He showed us a mountain, at no great distance, which he represented as being on the other side of a river, into which this empties itself.
*Thursday, June 13.*﻿—At an early hour of this morning the men began to cut a road, in order to carry the canoe and lading beyond the rapid; and by seven they were ready. That business was soon effected, and the canoe reladen, to proceed with the current which ran with great rapidity. In order to lighten her, it was my intention to walk with some of the people; but those in the boat with great earnestness requested me to embark, declaring, at the same time, that, if they perished, I should perish with them. I did not then imagine in how short a period their apprehension would be justified. We accordingly pushed off, and had proceeded but a very short way when the canoe struck, and notwithstanding all our exertions, the violence of the current was so great as to drive her sideways down the river, and break her by the first bar, when I instantly jumped into the water, and the men followed my example; but before we could set her straight, or stop her, we came to deeper water, so that we were obliged to re-embark with the utmost precipitation. One of the men who was not sufficiently active, was left to get on shore in the best manner in his power.

===Chinese===

六点半，两棵横倒河上的大树挡住了去路，好不容易才使独木舟没有撞上去。我们在这里卸货扎营。
天气阴冷，由于今天航程的种种情况逼得我们频频下水，而水又冷得像冰，我们几乎都冻儡了。有几个人先上岸为独木舟减载，因地面崎岖，费了很大力气才回到我们这里；他们到达时，天确实已经差不多黑了。我一上岸，便派两个人沿河下去了解一下河道情况，好让我判断明天可能等着我们的困难；他们带回的叙述令人胆寒——急流、倒木，还有巨石。在这里，向导明显露出不满的神情：他同我们一起冲下几处激流时吓坏了，表示很想回去。他指给我们看一座不远的山，说山在另一条河的对岸，我们这条河便注入那条河。
*6月13日，星期四*——今晨一早，人们便动手砍出一条路，以便把独木舟和货物搬过急流；七点，他们准备停当。这件事很快办完，独木舟重新装货，顺着湍急的水流前进。为了减轻舟的载重，我本打算同几个人一道步行；但船上的人恳切至极地请求我上船，同时声明：倘若他们遇难，我也当同他们一道罹难。我当时还想不到，他们的预感会在多么短的时间内应验。我们于是撜离河岸，才行出很短一段路，独木舟便撞上了，尽管我们拼尽全力，水流之猛还是把它打得横着顺河冲下，在第一道沙洲上撞破了；我立刻跳进水里，人们纷纷效仿；但还没等我们把舟摆正或截住，前面又是深水，我们只得手忙脚乱地重新上舟。有个动作不够麻利的人被落下，只好自己设法上岸。

===Original===

We had hardly regained our situations when we drove against a rock which shattered the stern of the canoe in such a manner, that it held only by the gunwales, so that the steersman could no longer keep his place. The violence of this stroke drove us to the opposite side of the river, which is but narrow, when the bow met with the same fate as the stern. At this moment the foreman seized on some branches of a small tree in the hope of bringing up the canoe, but such was their elasticity that, in a manner not easily described, he was jerked on shore in an instant, and with a degree of violence that threatened his destruction. But we had no time to turn from our own situation to enquire what had befallen him; for, in a few moments, we came across a cascade which broke several large holes in the bottom of the canoe, and started all the bars, except one behind the scooping seat. If this accident, however, had not happened, the vessel must have been irretrievably overset. The wreck becoming flat on the water, we all jumped out, while the steersman, who had been compelled to abandon his place, and had not recovered from his fright, called out to his companions to save themselves. My peremptory commands superseded the effects of his fear, and they all held fast to the wreck; to which fortunate resolution we owed our safety, as we should otherwise have been dashed against the rocks by the force of the water, or driven over the cascades. In this condition we were forced several hundred yards, and every yard on the verge of destruction; but, at length, we most fortunately arrived in shallow water and a small eddy, where we were enabled to make a stand, from the weight of the canoe resting on the stones, rather than from any exertions of our exhausted strength. For though our efforts were short, they were pushed to the utmost, as life or death depended on them.
This alarming scene, with all its terrors and dangers, occupied only a few minutes; and in the present suspension of it, we called to the people on shore to come to our assistance, and they immediately obeyed the summons. The foreman, however, was the first with us; he had escaped unhurt from the extraordinary jerk with which he was thrown out of the boat, and just as we were beginning to take our effects out of the water, he appeared to give his assistance.

===Chinese===

我们刚各归各位，便撞上一块岩石，把独木舟的船尾撞得粉碎，只靠舷缘连着，舵手已无法再稳住自己的位置。这一撞的猛劲把我们冲到河对面——河面本不宽——船头又遭到与船尾同样的命运。这时，头桨手一把抓住一棵小树的枝桠，想把独木舟拽停，但枝条弹性极大，以一种难以描述的方式把他瞬间甩上岸去，力道之大，几乎要了他的命。但我们无暇顾及自己的处境去问他出了什么事；因为不多时，我们便冲过一道瀑布，舟底被砸出几个大窟窿，除了舀水座后的一根，所有横档都松脱了。不过，若非出了这一事故，船必定要无可挽回地倾覆。残骸平摊在水上，我们全都跳了出去；被迫离位、惊魂未定的舵手高喊着让同伴们各自逃命。我严厉的号令压倒了他的恐惧，他们全都死死抓住残骸；正是这一幸运的决定救了我们的性命，否则我们必被水力甩到岩石上撞碎，或被冲下瀑布。在这种境况下，我们被冲出几百码，每一码都在毁灭的边缘；但最终我们万分幸运地到了浅水处和一个小回水湾，才得以站住脚——与其说靠我们气力耗尽后的挣扎，不如说靠独木舟的重量搁在石头上。因为尽管我们的努力为时甚短，却已用到了极限，生死全系于此。
这场骇人的景象，连同它一切的恐怖与危险，前后不过几分钟；在这一短暂的间歇里，我们呼喊岸上的人前来救援，他们立刻应声赶来。头一个到我们跟前的还是那位头桨手；他被甩出船的那一猛劲竟没有伤着，正当我们开始从水里往外捞取物品时，他赶来帮忙了。

===Original===

The Indians, when they saw our deplorable situation, instead of making the least effort to help us, sat down and gave vent to their tears. I was on the outside of the canoe, where I remained till everything was got on shore, in a state of great pain from the extreme cold of the water; so that at length, it was with difficulty I could stand, from the benumbed state of my limbs.
The loss was considerable and important, for it consisted of our whole stock of balls, and some of our furniture; but these considerations were forgotten in the impressions of our miraculous escape. Our first inquiry was after the absent man, whom in the first moment of danger, we had left to get on shore, and in a short time his appearance removed our anxiety. We had, however, sustained no personal injury of consequence, and my bruises seemed to be in the greater proportion.
All the different articles were now spread out to dry. The powder had fortunately received no damage, and all my instruments had escaped. Indeed, when my people began to recover from their alarm, and to enjoy a sense of safety, some of them, if not all, were by no means sorry for our late misfortune, from the hope that it must put a period to our voyage, particularly as we were without a canoe, and all the bullets sunk in the river. It did not, indeed, seem possible to them that we could proceed under these circumstances. I listened, however, to the observations that were made on the occasion without replying to them, till their panic was dispelled, and they had got themselves warm and comfortable, with an hearty meal, and rum enough to raise their spirits.
I then addressed them, by recommending them all to be thankful for their late very narrow escape. I also stated, that the navigation was not impracticable in itself, but from our ignorance of its course; and that our late experience would enable us to pursue our voyage with greater security. I brought to their recollection, that I did not deceive them, and that they were made acquainted with the difficulties and dangers they must expect to encounter, before they engaged to accompany me. I also urged the honour of conquering disasters, and the disgrace that would attend them on their return home, without having attained the object of the expedition.

===Chinese===

印第安人看到我们凄惨的处境，非但不作丝毫援救之想，反而坐下来放声大哭。我在独木舟外侧站着，直到一切都被弄上岸，一直泡在冰冷刺骨的水里，痛苦万分；到后来，我四肢冻得麻木，几乎站立不住。
损失重大而紧要：我们全部的弹丸，还有一部分日用器具都葬送了；但在死里逃生的震骇之中，这些都顾不上了。我们头一件挂念的是那个掉队的人，方才危急关头，我们丢下他让他自行上岸；不久他便露了面，我们的焦虑随之消解。我们都没有受什么要紧的伤，倒是我身上的淤伤似乎最多。
各式物件此刻都摊开晾晒。火药幸而没有受损，我的仪器也全都安然无恙。确实，当我的人们从惊惶中缓过来、享受到安全的滋味时，他们中间有些人——如果不是全体的话——对我们刚遭的不幸非但不觉遗憾，反而暗自庆幸，指望它必定会给这次航行画上句号，尤其我们既没了独木舟，弹丸又全沉进了河里。在他们看来，处在这种境地，我们实在不可能再往前走了。然而对就此发表的种种议论，我一言不发地听着，直到他们的恐慌消散，人人吃得热乎舒坦——一顿饱饭，外加足以往兴头上提神的朗姆酒。
然后我向他们讲话，劝大家为刚才死里逃生的大幸而感恩。我还说明，这段水道本身并非不可通行，只是我们对它的路线无知；而这次的教训会使我们更有把握地继续航程。我提醒他们，我并没有欺骗他们，在他们答应随我同行之前，就早已让他们明白将要面对的艰难与危险。我又以战胜灾祸的荣耀相激励，说倘若回到家乡而未达此行的目的，等待他们的将是耻辱。

===Original===

Nor did I fail to mention the courage and resolution which was the peculiar boast of the North men; and that I depended on them, at that moment, for the maintenance of their character. I quieted their apprehension as to the loss of the bullets, by bringing to their recollection that we still had shot from which they might be manufactured. I at the same time acknowledged the difficulty of restoring the wreck of the canoe, but confided in our skill and exertion to put it in such a state as would carry us on to where we might procure bark, and build a new one. In short, my harangue produced the desired effect, and a very general assent appeared to go wherever I should lead the way.
Various opinions were offered in the present posture of affairs, and it was rather a general wish that the wreck should be abandoned, and all the lading carried to the river, which our guide informed us was at no great distance, and in the vicinity of woods where he believed there was plenty of bark. This project seemed not to promise that certainty to which I looked in my present operations; besides, I had my doubts respecting the views of my guide, and consequently could not confide in the representation he made to me. I therefore dispatched two of the men at nine in the morning, with one of the young Indians, for I did not venture to trust the guide out of my sight, in search of bark, and to endeavor, if it were possible, in the course of the day, to penetrate to the great river, into which that before us discharges itself in the direction which the guide had communicated. I now joined my people in order to repair, as well as circumstances would admit, our wreck of a canoe, and I began to set them the example.
At noon I had an altitude, which gave 54° 23′ north latitude. At four in the afternoon I took time, with the hope that in the night I might obtain an observation of Jupiter, and his satellites, but I had not a sufficient horizon, from the propinquity of the mountains. The result of my calculation for the time was 1:32:28 slow apparent time.
It now grew late, and the people who had been sent on the excursion already mentioned, were not yet returned; about ten o’clock, however, I heard a man hallo, and I very gladly returned the signal.

===Chinese===

我也没有忘记提起勇气与坚毅——那是北方人引以为独有豪气的品格；并说此时此刻，我便指望他们来维护这份名声。为打消他们对弹丸损失的忧虑，我提醒他们还有霞弹，可以用来制造弹丸。同时我承认，要把独木舟的残骸修复确有困难，但我相信凭我们的手艺和干劲，定能把它修到足以载着我们前行，到能弄到树皮的地方造一只新的。总之，我这一番话收到了预期的效果，我指向哪里，众人便众口一词地愿意跟到哪里。
在眼下的局面下，众人提出了各种意见；比较普遍的愿望是弃掉残骸，把全部货载搬到那条河去——向导告诉我们，那条河距此不远，附近有树林，他相信那里树皮很多。这个方案在我看来不及我眼下打算的那样有把握；何况我对向导的用心本有疑虑，因而无法相信他对我说的话。于是早晨九点，我派了两个人，带上一名年轻的印第安人——因为我不敢让向导离开我的视线——去找树皮，并力图在当天之内，尽可能抵达那条大河：向导已告知方向，我们面前这条河便是注入那条大河的。我自己则同大家一道，尽力修整我们那独木舟的残骸，并率先动手给他们做出样子。
正午我测得高度，为北纬54°23′。下午四点我校了时，指望夜里能观测木星及其卫星，但群山相逼，地平线不够开阔。校时计算的结果是慢视时1:32:28。
天色渐晚，先前派出的人还未回来；约莫十点钟，我听到一声呼喊，便欣然回应了信号。

===Original===

In a short time our young Indian arrived with a small roll of indifferent bark: he was oppressed with fatigue and hunger, and his clothes torn to rags: he had parted with the other two men at sunset, who had walked the whole day, in a dreadful country, without procuring any good bark, or being able to get to the large river. His account of the river, on whose banks we were, could not be more unfavourable or discouraging; it had appeared to him to be little more than a succession of falls and rapids, with occasional interruptions of fallen trees.
Our guide became so dissatisfied and troubled in mind, that we could not obtain from him any regular account of the country before us. All we could collect from him was, that the river into which this empties itself, is but a branch of a large river, the great fork being at no great distance from the confluence of this; and that he knew of no lake, or large body of still water, in the vicinity of these rivers. To this account of the country, he added some strange, fanciful, but terrifying descriptions of the natives, similar to those which were mentioned in the former voyage.
We had an escape this day, which I must add to the many instances of good fortune which I experienced in this perilous expedition. The powder had been spread out, to the amount of eighty pounds weight, to receive the air; and, in this situation, one of the men carelessly and composedly walked across it with a lighted pipe in his mouth, but without any ill consequence resulting from such an act of criminal negligence. I need not add that one spark might have put a period to all my anxiety and ambition.
I observed several trees and plants on the banks of this river, which I had not seen to the north of the latitude 52° such as the cedar, maple, hemlock, etc. At this time the water rose fast, and passed on with the rapidity of an arrow shot from a bow.
*Friday 14.*﻿—The weather was fine, clear, and warm, and at an early hour of the morning we resumed our repair of the canoe. At half past seven our two men returned hungry and cold, not having tasted food, or enjoyed the least repose for twenty-four hours, with their clothes torn into tatters, and their skin lacerated, in passing through the woods.

===Chinese===

不多时，我们的年轻印第安人带着一小卷质地平平的树皮回来了：他又累又饿，衣衫褴褛；他是日落时同另外两人分手的——那两人走了一整天，穿过一片可怕的地方，既没弄到好树皮，也无法抵达那条大河。他对我们所在这条河沿岸情形的叙述，再不利、再叫人灰心不过了：在他看来，这条河几乎就是接连不断的瀑布和激流，间或被倒木截断。
我们的向导变得极为不满、心烦意乱，我们无法从他那里得到关于前方地带的任何连贯的陈述。我们所能打听到的只是：这条河所注入的那条河，只是一条大河的支流，大河的大分岔口离两河汇合处不远；在这一带河流附近，他不知道有任何湖泊或大的静水。在叙述这些情况之外，他还添上一些关于土著的怪诞、离奇却令人胆寒的描述，同前一次航行中提到过的那些相仿。
这一天我们还有一桩脱险的事，必须记入我在这次险象环生的远征中所经历的许多好运之列。火药当时摊开晾着，约有八十磅重；就在这个当口，有个人嘴里夻着点着的烟斗，漫不经心、若无其事地从火药上走了过去，所幸这一近乎犯罪的疏忽竟没有引起任何恶果。不必说，一粒火星便足以了结我全部的焦虑与抱负。
我在这条河两岸观察到几种北纬52°以北未曾见过的树木和植物，如雪松、槭树、铁杉等。此时河水猛涨，水流如离弦之箭。
*14日，星期五*——天气晴朗温暖，一早我们便继续修整独木舟。七点半，我们的两个人回来了，又冷又饿，二十四小时不曾进食、不曾合眼，衣裳被扯成了碎条，皮肤在穿越树林时划得满是口子。

===Original===

Their account was the same as that brought by the Indian, with this exception, that they had reason to think they saw the river, or branch which our guide had mentioned: but they were of opinion that from the frequent obstructions in this river, we should have to carry the whole way to it, through a dreadful country, where much time and labour would be required to open a passage through it.
Discouraging as these accounts were, they did not, however, interrupt for a moment the task in which we were engaged, of repairing the canoe; and this work we contrived to complete by the conclusion of the day. The bark which was brought by the Indian, with some pieces of oilcloth, and plenty of gum, enabled us to put our shattered vessel in a condition to answer our present purposes. The guide, who has been mentioned as manifesting continual signs of dissatisfaction, now assumed an air of contentment, which I attributed to a smoke that was visible in the direction of the river; as he naturally expected, if we should fall in with any natives, which was now very probable, from such a circumstance, that he should be released from a service which he had found so irksome and full of danger. I had an observation at noon, which made our latitude 54° 23′ 48″ north. I also took time, and found it slow apparent time 1:38:44
*Saturday, June 15.*﻿—The weather continued the same as the preceding day, and according to the directions which I had previously given, my people began at a very early hour to open a road, through which we might carry a part of our lading; as I was fearful of risking the whole of it in the canoe, in its present weak state, and in a part of the river which is full of shoals and rapids. Four men were employed to conduct her, lightened as she was of twelve packages. They passed several dangerous places, and met with various obstructions, the current of the river being frequently stopped by rafts of drift wood, and fallen trees, so that after fourteen hours hard labour we had not made more than three miles. Our course was southeast by east, and as we had not met with any accident, the men appeared to feel a renewed courage to continue their voyage. In the morning, however, one of the crew, whose name was Beauchamp, peremptorily refused to embark in the canoe.

===Chinese===

他们的说法同那名印第安人带回的一样，只有一点例外：他们有理由认为自己望见了向导提到的那条河或支流；但他们认为，鉴于这条河上障碍频繁，我们一路都得靠搬运才能抵达那里，而且要穿过一片可怕的地方，开出通路来需耗费大量的时间和劳力。
这些说法尽管叫人泄气，却一刻也没有中断我们手头修理独木舟的活计；到这天结束时，这活儿总算完成了。那名印第安人带来的树皮，加上几块油布和充足的树胶，使我们得以把破船修到能应付眼前用途的状态。前面提到过，向导一直流露出不满的迹象，此刻却摆出一副心满意足的神气，我把它归因于河那个方向可见的一缕烟；因为倘若我们遇上任何土著——眼下从这迹象看很有可能——他自然指望能从这份他已觉得如此劳苦、如此凶险的差事中解脱出来。正午我作了一次观测，测得我们的纬度为北纬54°23′48″。我又校了时，得慢视时1:38:44。
*6月15日，星期六*——天气同前一天一样，依照我先前的指示，我的人一早便动手开辟一条路，好搬运一部分货载；因为我不放心让独木舟在眼下这般虚弱的状态下、在这满是浅滩和激流的河段载运全部货物。四个人负责操纵它，减去了十二件包裹的载重。他们闯过几处险地，遇到种种阻碍——河水时常被成筏的浮木和倒木截断——以致苦干十四个小时，我们才行进了不到三英里。我们的航向是东南偏东，由于没有出事故，人们似乎重新鼓起了继续航行的勇气。然而清晨，船员中一个名叫博尚（Beauchamp）的人断然拒绝登上独木舟。

===Original===

This being the first example of absolute disobedience which had yet appeared during the course of our expedition, I should not have passed it over without taking some very severe means to prevent a repetition of it; but as he had the general character of a simple fellow, among his companions, and had been frightened out of what little sense he possessed, by our late dangers, I rather preferred to consider him as unworthy of accompanying us, and to represent him as an object of ridicule and contempt for his pusillanimous behaviour; though, in fact, he was a very useful, active, and laborious man.
At the close of the day we assembled round a blazing fire; and the whole party, being enlivened with the usual beverage which I supplied on these occasions, forgot their fatigues and apprehensions; nor did they fail to anticipate the pleasure they should enjoy in getting clear of their present difficulties, and gliding onwards with a strong and steady stream, which our guide had described as the characteristic of the large river we soon expected to enter.
*Sunday, June 16.*﻿—The fine weather continued, and we began our work, as we had done the preceding day; some were occupied in opening a road, others were carrying, and the rest employed in conducting the canoe. I was of the first party, and soon discovered that we had encamped about half a mile above several falls, over which we could not attempt to run the canoe, lightened even as she was. This circumstance rendered it necessary that the road should be made sufficiently wide to admit the canoe to pass; a tedious and toilsome work. In running her down a rapid above the falls, a hole was broken in her bottom, which occasioned a considerable delay, as we were destitute of the materials necessary for her effectual reparation. On my being informed of this misfortune, I returned, and ordered Mr. Mackay, with two Indians, to quit their occupation in making the road, and endeavour to penetrate to the great river, according to the direction which the guide had communicated, without paying any attention to the course of the river before us.
When the people had repaired the canoe in the best manner they were able, we conducted her to the head of the falls; she was then unloaded and taken out of the water, when we carried her for a considerable distance through a low, swampy country.

===Chinese===

这是我们远征以来出现的第一桩公然抗命的先例，按理我不该轻轻放过而不采取十分严厉的手段，以防再有类似事情发生；但他在同伴中间向来有头脑简单的名声，又已被连番危险吓掉了仅有的一点理智，我宁愿把他看作不配与我们同行的人，把他那怯懦行径当作讥笑和鄙夷的对象；尽管事实上，他是个非常得力、勤快、肯干的人。
这天结束时，我们围坐在一堆旺火旁；全队人喝着我在这种场合照例供应的酒水，精神一振，忘却了疲劳与忧惧；他们还纷纷憧憬起摆脱眼前困境之后的快意——顺着向导描述的那条大河所特有的强劲而平稳的水流，一路顺流而下；我们不久便可望驶入那条大河了。
*6月16日，星期日*——晴好的天气持续着，我们同前一天一样开始干活：一些人开路，一些人搬运，其余的人操纵独木舟。我在第一批人里，很快便发现我们的营地位于几处瀑布上方约半英里处，这些瀑布连减轻了载重的独木舟也不能冒险冲下去。这就必须把路开得足够宽，好让独木舟通过；这是一件又磨人又费力的活。在把舟放下一道瀑布上游的激流时，舟底破了一个洞，造成相当大的耽搁，因为我们缺少把它彻底修好的材料。我得知这一不幸，便返回去，命令麦凯先生带两名印第安人放下开路的活计，尽力按向导所示的方向抵达那条大河，不必顾及我们面前这条河的走向。
人们把独木舟尽其所能修好之后，我们将它护送到瀑布的上头；随后卸了载，把舟从水里抬出来，抬着它穿过一片低洼多沼的乡野走了相当远的一段路。

===Original===

I appointed four men to this laborious office, which they executed at the peril of their lives, for the canoe was now become so heavy, from the additional quantity of bark and gum necessary to patch her up, that two men could not carry her more than an hundred yards, without being relieved; and as their way lay through deep mud, which was rendered more difficult by the roots and prostrate trunks of trees, they were every moment in danger of falling; and beneath such a weight, one false step might have been attended with fatal consequences. The other two men and myself followed as fast as we could, with the lading. Thus did we toil till seven o’clock in the evening, to get to the termination of the road that had been made in the morning. Here Mr. Mackay and the Indian joined us, after having been at the river, which they represented as rather large. They had also observed, that the lower part of the river before us was so full of fallen wood, that the attempt to clear a passage through it, would be an unavailing labour. The country through which they had passed was morass, and almost impenetrable wood. In passing over one of the embarras, our dog, which was following them, fell in, and it was with very great difficulty that he was saved, as the current had carried him under the drift. They brought with them two geese, which had been shot in the course of their expedition. To add to our perplexities and embarrassments, we were persecuted by mosquitoes and sandflies, through the whole of the day.
The extent of our journey was not more than two miles southeast; and so much fatigue and pain had been suffered in the course of it, that my people, as might be expected, looked forward to a continuance of it with discouragement and dismay. I was, indeed, informed that murmurs prevailed among them, of which, however, I took no notice. When we were assembled together for the night, I gave each of them a dram, and in a short time they retired to the repose which they so much required. We could discover the termination of the mountains at a considerable distance on either side of us, which, according to my conjecture, marked the course of the great river. On the mountains to the east there were several fires, as their smokes were very visible to us. Excessive heat prevailed throughout the day.
*Monday, June 17.

===Chinese===

我指派四个人担任这项苦差，他们是冒着生命危险完成的，因为独木舟此刻由于补船所需的树皮和树胶，已经变得非常沉重，两个人抬不了一百步就得换人；加之一路尽是深泥，树根和倒卧的树干更增加了难度，他们时时刻刻都有跌倒的危险；而在那样的重压之下，一步踏错便可能有性命之虞。另外两个人和我尽可能紧随其后搬运货物。我们就这样一直苦干到晚上七点，才到达早晨开辟的那条路的尽头。麦凯先生和那名印第安人在这里同我们会合，他们已到过那条河，说那条河相当宽大。他们还注意到，我们这条河的下游段倒木密布，想从中开出一条通路将是徒劳无功的苦工。他们穿越的地方尽是沼泽和几乎无法通行的密林。在跨过一处浮木淤塞时，跟着他们的狗掉了进去，费了非常大的劲才把它救出来，因为水流已把它卷到了浮木底下。他们带回两只雁，是途中打到的。更添我们烦难困顿的是，整整一天，蚊子和蚋虫都在折磨我们。
这一天的路程不过东南向二英里；一路所受的疲劳和痛苦实在太多，可以想见，我的人怀着沮丧和惊惶展望它的延续。我确实得知他们中间怨声四起，但我没有理会。夜里聚齐时，我给每人斟了一杯酒，不久他们便去享受那急需的休息了。我们能在两侧相当远处望见群山的尽头，依我的推测，那正标示着大河的走向。东边的山上有几处火头，烟柱对我们非常显眼。全天酷热难当。
*6月17日，星期一

===Original===

*﻿—Having sat up till twelve last night, which had been my constant practice since we had taken our present guide, I awoke Mr. Mackay to watch him in turn. I then laid down to rest, and at three I was awakened to be informed that he had deserted. Mr. Mackay, with whom I was displeased on this occasion, and the Cancre, accompanied by the dog, went in search of him, but he had made his escape: a design which he had for some time meditated, though I had done everything in my power to induce him to remain with me.
This misfortune did not produce any relaxation in our exertions. At an early hour of the morning we were all employed in cutting a passage of three quarters of a mile, through which we carried our canoe and cargo, when we put her into the water with her lading, but in a very short time were stopped by the driftwood, and were obliged to land and carry. In short, we pursued our alternate journeys, by land and water, till noon, when we could proceed no further, from the various small unnavigable channels into which the river branched in every direction; and no other mode of getting forward now remained for us, but by cutting a road across a neck of land. I accordingly dispatched two men to ascertain the exact distance, and we employed the interval of their absence in unloading and getting the canoe out of the water. It was eight in the evening when we arrived at the bank of the great river. This journey was three quarters of a mile east-northeast, through a continued swamp, where, in many places, we waded up to the middle of our thighs. Our course in the small river was about southeast by east three miles. At length we enjoyed, after all our toil and anxiety, the inexpressible satisfaction of finding ourselves on the bank of a navigable river, on the west side of the first great range of mountains.

===Chinese===

*——昨夜我一直守到十二点——自带上这位向导以来，这是我一贯的做法——然后唤醒麦凯先生轮班看守他。我随即躺下休息，三点钟被叫醒，得知向导已经逃走了。我对此事心怀不满，便连同麦凯先生和坎克勒，带着狗出去搜寻他，但他已经脱身了：这是他蓄谋已久的打算，尽管我已千方百计想让他留在我身边。
这一不幸并没有使我们有所松懈。一早，我们全都动手砍出一条四分之三英里长的通道，把独木舟和货物搬运过去，随后让舟载着货下水，但没过多久便被浮木拦住，只得登岸再搬。总之，我们就这样水陆交替地赶路，直到正午，再也无法前进——河道向四面八方分出许多无法通航的小汊；此刻除了横穿一处地峡砍出一条路，再无别的办法向前。我于是派两个人去测准距离，我们利用他们离开的间隙卸货，把独木舟从水里弄出来。晚上八点，我们到达大河的岸边。这段路为东东北向四分之三英里，穿行在连绵的沼泽中，许多地方我们一直蹚到大腿深。我们在小河中的航程约为东南偏东三英里。历经一切劳苦与焦灼之后，我们终于享受到那种难以言表的满足：发现自己站在一条可通航的大河岸边，在第一道大山脉的西侧。

### VII / 七
===Original===

*Tuesday, June 18.*﻿—It rained throughout the night and till seven in the morning; nor was I sorry that the weather gave me an excuse for indulging my people with that additional rest, which their fatigues, during the last three days, rendered so comfortable to them. Before eight, however, we were on the water, and driven on by a strong current, when we steered east-southeast half a mile, southwest by south half a mile, south-southeast half a mile, southwest half a mile, went round to northwest half a mile, backed south-southeast three quarters of a mile, south-southwest half a mile, south by east a quarter of a mile, and southwest by south three quarters of a mile. Here the water had fallen considerably, so that several mud and sandbanks were visible. There was also a hill ahead, west-southwest.
The weather was so hazy that we could not see across the river, which is here about two hundred yards wide. We now proceeded south by west one third of a mile, when we saw a considerable quantity of beaver work along the banks, north-northwest half a mile, southwest by west one mile and a half, south-southwest one third of a mile, west by south one third of a mile, south by east half a mile. Mountains rose on the left, immediately above the river, whose summits were covered with snow; southwest half a mile, south a quarter of a mile, southeast one third of a mile, south-southwest half a mile. Here are several islands; we then veered to west by south a third of a mile, south-southeast a sixth of a mile. On the right, the land is high, rocky, and covered with wood; west-southwest one mile; a small river running in from the southeast; southwest half a mile, south three quarters of a mile, southwest half a mile, south by west half a mile. Here a rocky point protrudes from the left, and narrows the river to a hundred yards; southeast half a mile, east by south one eighth of a mile. The current now was very strong, but perfectly safe; southeast by south an eighth of a mile, west by north one third of a mile, south by west a twelfth of a mile, southwest one fourth of a mile. Here the high land terminates on one side of the river, while rocks rise to a considerable height immediately above the other, and the channel widens to a hundred and fifty yards, west by south one mile.

===Chinese===

*6月18日，星期二*——雨下了整夜，一直下到早晨七点；我并不为此难过，因为天气给了我一个借口，让我的人们多歇一会儿——过去三天的劳累使他们太需要这种休息了。不过八点前我们已上了水，被强劲的水流推着前进，航向为东南东半英里，西南偏南半英里，南南东半英里，西南半英里，绕转至西北半英里，倒退回南南东四分之三英里，南南西半英里，南偏东四分之一英里，再西南偏南四分之三英里。这里水位已退落不少，露出了几处泥洲和沙洲。前方西西南方向还有一座山。
雾气浓得连河对面都看不见，此处河宽约二百码。我们接着向南偏西行三分之一英里，沿岸见到大量海狸的工程，又北西北半英里，西南偏西一英里半，南南西三分之一英里，西偏南三分之一英里，南偏东半英里。左边紧贴河岸隆起几座山，山顶积雪覆盖；西南半英里，正南四分之一英里，东南三分之一英里，南南西半英里。这里有几座岛；我们随后折向西偏南三分之一英里，南南东六分之一英里。右侧的土地高峻多岩，覆满林木；西西南一英里；一条小河从东南汇入；西南半英里，正南四分之三英里，西南半英里，南偏西半英里。这里一处岩石地角从左边探出，把河面挤窄到一百码；东南半英里，东偏南八分之一英里。此刻水流很急，但十分安全；东南偏南八分之一英里，西偏北三分之一英里，南偏西十二分之一英里，西南四分之一英里。这里高地在一岸到了尽头，而岩石在另一岸拔地而起，相当高峻，河道拓宽到一百五十码；西偏南一英里。

===Original===

The river now narrows again between rocks of a moderate height, north-northeast an eighth of a mile, veered to southwest an eighth of a mile, south and southwest half a mile. The country appeared to be low, as far as I could judge of it from the canoe, as the view is confined by woods at the distance of about a hundred yards from the banks. Our course continued west by north two miles, north half a mile, northwest a quarter of a mile, southwest two miles, northwest three quarters of a mile; when a ridge of high land appeared in this direction; west one mile. A small river flowed in from the north; south a quarter of a mile, northwest half a mile, south-southwest two miles and a half, southeast three quarters of a mile; a rivulet lost itself in the main stream, west-northwest half a mile. Here the current slackened, and we proceeded south-southwest three quarters of a mile, southwest three quarters of a mile, south by east three quarters of a mile, southeast by east one mile, when it veered gradually to west-northwest half a mile; the river being full of islands. We proceeded due north, with little current, the river presenting a beautiful sheet of water for a mile and a half, southwest by west one mile, west-northwest one mile, when it veered round to southeast one mile, west by north one mile, southeast one mile, west by north three quarters of a mile, south one eighth of a mile, when we came to an Indian cabin of late erection. Here was the great fork, of which our guide had informed us, and it appeared to be the largest branch from the southeast. It is about half a mile in breadth, and assumes the form of a lake. The current was very slack, and we got into the middle of the channel, when we steered west, and sounded in sixteen feet water.
A ridge of high land now stretched on, as it were, across our present direction: this course was three miles. We then proceeded west-southwest two miles, and sounded in twenty-four feet water. Here the river narrowed and the current increased. We then continued our course north-northwest three quarters of a mile, a small river falling in from the northeast.

===Chinese===

河道此刻又在中等高度的岩石之间收窄，北东北八分之一英里，折向西南八分之一英里，正南与西南半英里。就我在独木舟上所能判断的，这一带乡野显得低平，因为视野被距岸约一百码处的林木挡住了。我们的航路继续为西偏北二英里，正北半英里，西北四分之一英里，西南二英里，西北四分之三英里；此时这个方向上出现一道高地；正西一英里。一条小河从北面流入；正南四分之一英里，西北半英里，南南西二英里半，东南四分之三英里；一条小溪消失在干流里，西西北半英里。这里水流放缓，我们向南南西行四分之三英里，西南四分之三英里，南偏东四分之三英里，东南偏东一英里，随后河道渐渐折向西西北半英里；河中满是岛屿。我们向正北行进，水流微小，河面呈现出一泓漂亮的水，绵延一英里半；西南偏西一英里，西西北一英里，随后折转东南一英里，西偏北一英里，东南一英里，西偏北四分之三英里，正南八分之一英里，这时我们来到一座新近搭建的印第安小屋前。这里便是向导向我们通报过的大分岔口，最大的支流似乎从东南方而来。它约半英里宽，湖沼一般。水流非常缓慢，我们进入河道中央，向西行驶，测得水深十六英尺。
一道高地此刻仿佛横亘在我们眼前的方向上：这段航程三英里。我们随后向西南西行二英里，测得水深二十四英尺。这里河道收窄，水流加急。我们接着继续北西北四分之三英里，一条小河从东北汇入。

===Original===

It now veered to south by west one mile and a quarter, west-southwest four miles and a half, west by north one mile and a quarter, northwest by west one mile, west a mile and a quarter: the land was high on both sides, and the river narrowed to an hundred and fifty, or two hundred yards; northwest three quarters of a mile, southwest by south two miles and a half: here its breadth again increased; south by west one mile, west-southwest half a mile, southwest by south three miles, south-southeast one mile, with a small river running in from the left, south with a strong current one mile, then east three quarters of a mile, southwest one mile, south-southeast a mile and a half; the four last distances being a continual rapid, southwest by west one mile, east-northeast a mile and a half, east-southeast one mile, where a small river flowed in on the right; southwest by south two miles and a half, when another small river appeared from the same quarter; south by east half a mile and southwest by west one mile and a quarter: here we landed for the night. When we had passed the last river we observed smoke rising from it, as if produced by fires that had been fresh lighted; I therefore concluded that there were natives on its banks: but I was unwilling to fatigue my people, by pulling back against the current in order to go in search of them.
This river appeared, from its high watermark, to have fallen no more than one foot, while the smaller branch, from a similar measurement, had sunk two feet and a half. On our entering it, we saw a flock of ducks which were entirely white, except the bill and part of the wings. The weather was cold and raw throughout the day, and the wind southwest. We saw a smoke rising in columns from many parts of the woods, and I should have been more anxious to see the natives, if there had been any person with me who could have introduced me to them; but as that object could not be then attained without considerable loss of time, I determined to pursue the navigation while it continued to be so favourable, and to wait till my return, if no very convenient opportunity offered in the meantime, to engage an intercourse with them.
*Wednesday, June 19.*﻿—The morning was foggy, and at three we were on the water. At half past that hour, our course was east by south three quarters of a mile, a small river flowing in from the right.

===Chinese===

河道随即折向南偏西一英里又四分之一，西南西四英里半，西偏北一英里又四分之一，西北偏西一英里，正西一英里又四分之一：两岸土地高峻，河面收窄到一百五十或二百码；西北四分之三英里，西南偏南二英里半：这里河宽又增大了；南偏西一英里，西南西半英里，西南偏南三英里，南南东一英里，其间一条小河从左边汇入，正南顺着急流行一英里，再正东四分之三英里，西南一英里，南南东一英里半；这最后四段是连绵不断的激流；西南偏西一英里，东东北一英里半，东南东一英里，一条小河从右边汇入；西南偏南二英里半，另一条小河又从同一方向出现；南偏东半英里，西南偏西一英里又四分之一：我们在这里登岸过夜。驶过最后那条河时，我们看见河边升起烟来，仿佛是新点的火产生的；我因此断定岸边有土著；但我不愿让我的人们逆流划回去寻找他们，把他们累着。
从高水位线看，这条河的水位只落了不过一英尺，而那条较小的支流，按同样的测算，已退落了两英尺半。我们驶入这条河时，看见一群鸭，除喙和部分翅膀外通体雪白。全天天气阴冷，风从西南来。我们看见林中许多地方升起一道道烟柱；倘若我身边有能为我引见的人，我本会更急切地想见到土著；但由于这件事在当时非花费相当多的时间不能办到，我便决定趁航行条件如此有利之际继续赶路，若中途没有非常方便的机会，就等回来时再设法同他们交往。
*6月19日，星期三*——早晨有雾，三点我们上了水。到三点半，航向为东偏南四分之三英里，一条小河从右边流入。

===Original===

We then proceeded south by east half a mile, and south-southwest a mile and a half. During the last distance, clouds of thick smoke rose from the woods, that darkened the atmosphere, accompanied with a strong odour of the gum of cypress and the spruce-fir. Our courses continued to be southwest a mile and a quarter, northwest by west three quarters of a mile, south-southeast a mile and a quarter, east three quarters of a mile, southwest one mile, west by south three quarters of a mile, southeast by south three quarters of a mile, south by west half a mile, west by south three quarters of a mile, south by west two miles and a half. In the last course there was an island, and it appeared to me, that the main channel of the river had formerly been on the other side of it. The banks were here composed of high white cliffs, crowned with pinnacles in very grotesque shapes. We continued to steer southeast by south a mile and a half, south by east half a mile, east one mile and a quarter, southeast by east one mile, south by east three quarters of a mile, southeast by east one mile, south-southeast half a mile, east one mile and a quarter, south by east half a mile, east a mile and half, south-southeast three miles, and southwest three quarters of a mile. In the last course the rocks contracted in such a manner on both sides of the river, as to afford the appearance of the upper part of a fall or cataract. Under this apprehension we landed on the left shore, where we found a kind of footpath, imperfectly traced, through which we conjectured that the natives occasionally passed with their canoes and baggage. On examining the course of the river, however, there did not appear to be any fall as we expected; but the rapids were of a considerable length and impassable for a light canoe.
