We had therefore no alternative but to widen the road so as to admit the passage of our canoe, which was now carried with great difficulty; as from her frequent repairs, and not always of the usual materials, her weight was such, that she cracked and broke on the shoulders of the men who bore her. The labour and fatigue of this undertaking, from eight till twelve, beggars all description, when we at length conquered this afflicting passage, of about half a mile, over a rocky and most rugged hill. Our course was south-southwest.

===Chinese===

我们随后向南偏东行半英里，再南南西一英里半。行过最后这段时，一团团浓烟从林中升起，遮暗了天空，还带着一股浓烈的柏树和云杉树脂的气味。我们的航向继续为西南一英里又四分之一，西北偏西四分之三英里，南南东一英里又四分之一，正东四分之三英里，西南一英里，西偏南四分之三英里，东南偏南四分之三英里，南偏西半英里，西偏南四分之三英里，南偏西二英里半。最后一段航程中有一座岛，依我看，河的主航道从前是在岛的另一侧。这里的河岸由高高的白峭壁构成，顶上冠着形状极为怪异的尖岩。我们继续朝东南偏南行一英里半，南偏东半英里，正东一英里又四分之一，东南偏东一英里，南偏东四分之三英里，东南偏东一英里，南南东半英里，正东一英里又四分之一，南偏东半英里，正东一英里半，南南东三英里，再西南四分之三英里。在最后这段航程里，两侧的岩石紧紧夹拢，看去像是一道瀑布或大瀑布的上段。出于这种担忧，我们在左岸登了陆，发现一条隐约踩出的小径，我们猜想土著们偶尔会带着独木舟和行李从这里通过。然而察看河道后发现，并不像我们预想的那样有什么瀑布；只是激流相当长，轻便独木舟也无法通过。我们别无办法，只好把路拓宽，好让我们的独木舟通过；此时抬船已非常吃力：由于屡次修补，用的又不总是惯常的材料，它已沉重得在抬船人的肩上咯吱作响、频频开裂。这项工程从八点干到十二点，那份劳苦简直无法形容，我们终于翻过了这段约半英里、遍布岩石、崎岖至极的山路，征服了这个折磨人的关口。我们的走向是南南西。

===Original===

Here I took a meridian altitude which gave me 53° 42′ 20″ north latitude. We, however, lost some time to put our canoe in a condition to carry us onwards. Our course was south a quarter of a mile to the next carrying-place; which was nothing more than a rocky point about twice the length of the canoe. From the extremity of this point to the rocky and almost perpendicular bank that rose on the opposite shore, is not more than forty or fifty yards. The great body of water, at the same time tumbling in successive cascades along the first carrying-place, rolls through this narrow passage in a very turbid current, and full of whirlpools. On the banks of the river there was great plenty of wild onions, which when mixed up with our pemmican was a great improvement of it; though they produced a physical effect on our appetites, which was rather inconvenient to the state of our provisions.
Here we embarked, and steered southeast by east three quarters of a mile. We now saw a smoke on the shore; but before we could reach land the natives had deserted their camp, which appeared to be erected for no more than two families. My two Indians were instantly dispatched in search of them, and, by following their tracks, they soon overtook them; but their language was mutually unintelligible; and all attempts to produce a friendly communication were fruitless. They no sooner perceived my young men than they prepared their bows and arrows, and made signs for them not to advance; and they thought it prudent to desist from proceeding, though not before the natives had discharged five arrows at them, which, however, they avoided, by means of the trees. When they returned with this account, I very much regretted that I had not accompanied them; and as these people could not be at any very great distance, I took Mr. Mackay, and one of the Indians with me in order to overtake them; but they had got so far it would have been imprudent in me to have followed them. My Indians, who, I believe, were terrified at the manner in which these natives received them, informed me, that, besides their bows, arrows, and spears, they were armed with long knives, and that they accompanied their strange antics with menacing actions and loud shoutings. On my return, I found my people indulging their curiosity in examining the bags and baskets which the natives had left behind them.

===Chinese===

我在这里测了子午高度，得北纬53°42′20″。不过我们耽搁了一些时间，把独木舟修整到能载我们继续上路。我们的航向是向南四分之一英里，到下一个搬运点；那不过是一处岩石地角，约有两倍独木舟的长度。从地角的尽头到对岸拔起的岩石、近乎垂直的河岸，不过四五十码。与此同时，大股的水沿着第一个搬运点接连翻落成一道道瀑布，卷着浑浊的水流、夹着一个个漩涡滚过这段狭窄的水道。河岸上野生洋葱极多，掺进我们的干肉饼（pemmican）里，大为增味；只是它们对我们食欲的促进作用，对我们口粮的状况未免有些不便。
我们在这里登船，向东南偏东行四分之三英里。这时我们看见岸上有一缕烟；但还没等我们靠岸，土著已弃营而去，那营地看去不过住着两户人家。我的两名印第安人立即被派去寻找他们，循着踪迹很快追上了；但彼此的语言不通；一切建立友好交往的尝试都是徒劳。那些人一见我的年轻人便张弓搭箭，做手势叫他们不得上前；他们觉得还是止步为妙，尽管土著已朝他们射出五箭——好在他们借树木躲开了。他们带回这一消息后，我深悔没有同他们一起去；由于这些人走不了太远，我便带上麦凯先生和一名印第安人去追赶；但他们已经走得太远，再追下去未免轻率。我相信我的印第安人已被这些土著迎接他们的架势吓坏了，他们告诉我，那些人除弓、箭、矛之外还佩着长刀，一边做出种种怪异的姿态，一边带着威胁的动作高声喊叫。我回来时，发现我的人正兴致勃勃地翻看土著留下的袋子和篮子。

===Original===

Some of them contained their fishing tackle, such as nets, lines, etc., others of a smaller size were filled with a red earth, with which they paint themselves. In several of the bags there were also sundry articles of which we did not know the use. I prevented my men from taking any of them; and for a few articles of mere curiosity, which I took myself, I left such things in exchange as would be much more useful to their owners.
At four we left this place, proceeding with the stream southeast three quarters of a mile, east-southeast one mile, south three quarters of a mile, south-southwest one mile, south by east three quarters of a mile, south-southeast one mile, south-southwest two miles, south-southeast three miles and a quarter, east by north one mile, south-southeast one mile and a quarter, with a rapid, south-southwest three quarters of a mile, south one mile and a half, southeast one mile and a quarter, south three quarters of a mile, and south-southeast one mile and a half. At half past seven we landed for the night, where a small river flowed in from the right. The weather was showery, accompanied with several loud claps of thunder. The banks were overshadowed by lofty firs, and wide-spreading cedars.
*Thursday, June 20.*﻿—The morning was foggy, and at half past four we proceeded with a south wind, southeast by east two miles, south-southeast two miles and a half, and south-southwest two miles. The fog was so thick, that we could not see the length of our canoe, which rendered our progress dangerous, as we might have come suddenly upon a cascade or violent rapid. Our next course was west-northwest two miles and a half, which comprehended a rapid. Being close in with the left bank of the river, we perceived two red deer at the very edge of the water: we killed one of them, and wounded the other, which was very small. We now landed, and the Indians followed the wounded animal, which they soon caught, and would have shot another in the woods, if our dog, who followed them, had not disturbed it. From the number of their tracks it appeared that they abounded in this country. They are not so large as the elk of the Peace River, but are the real red deer, which I never saw in the north, though I have been told that they are to be found in great numbers in the plains along the Red, or Assiniboin River.

===Chinese===

其中一些装着他们的渔具，如网、钓线等；另一些小些的装满红色泥土，是他们用来涂身的。有几只袋子里还有种种我们不知用途的物件。我不许我的人拿走任何东西；我自己取了几件纯粹出于好奇的东西，留下的物件对它们的主人可要有用得多。
四点，我们离开此地，顺流朝东南行四分之三英里，东南东一英里，正南四分之三英里，南南西一英里，南偏东四分之三英里，南南东一英里，南南西二英里，南南东三英里又四分之一，东偏北一英里，南南东一英里又四分之一（其间一道激流），南南西四分之三英里，正南一英里半，东南一英里又四分之一，正南四分之三英里，再南南东一英里半。七点半，我们在一条小河从右边汇入处登岸过夜。天气阵雨不断，还伴着几声响雷。两岸被高耸的冷杉和铺展的雪松遮蔽着。
*6月20日，星期四*——早晨有雾，四点半我们乘着南风上路，东南偏东二英里，南南东二英里半，南南西二英里。雾浓得连独木舟那么长的距离都看不清，行进因此很危险，说不定会猛然撞上一道瀑布或凶猛的激流。我们的下一段航向是西西北二英里半，其中包括一道激流。紧贴着河的左岸行进时，我们发现两只赤鹿正在水边：我们打死了一只，打伤了另一只，那只是很小的一只。我们随即登岸，印第安人去追那受伤的牲口，很快就逮住了；若不是我们的狗跟着他们惊动了林中另一只，他们本还能再打到一只。从足迹的数量看，这种鹿在这一带似乎很多。它们不及和平河（Peace River）的麋鹿大，却是真正的赤鹿；我在北方从未见过，尽管听说在红河即阿西尼博因河（Assiniboin River）沿岸的平原上为数甚多。

===Original===

The bark had been stripped off many of the spruce trees, and carried away, as I presumed, by the natives, for the purpose of covering their cabins. We now got the venison on board, and continued our voyage southwest one mile, south a mile and a half, and west one mile. Here the country changed its appearance; the banks were but of a moderate height, from whence the ground continued gradually rising to a considerable distance, covered with poplars and cypresses, but without any kind of underwood. There are also several low points which the river, that is here about three hundred yards in breadth, sometimes overflows, and are shaded with the liard, the soft birch, the spruce, and the willow. For some distance before we came to this part of the river, our view was confined within very rugged, irregular, and lofty banks, which were varied with the poplar, different kinds of spruce fir, small birch trees, cedars, alders, and several species of the willow. Our next course was southwest by west six miles, when we landed at a deserted house, which was the only Indian habitation of this kind that I had seen on this side of Michilimackinac. It was about thirty feet long and twenty wide, with three doors, three feet high by one foot and an half in breadth. From this and other circumstances, it appears to have been constructed for three families. There were also three fireplaces, at equal distances from each other; and the beds were on either side of them. Behind the beds was a narrow space, in the form of a manger, and somewhat elevated, which was appropriated to the purpose of keeping fish. The wall of the house, which was five feet in height, was formed of very strait spruce timbers, brought close together, and laid into each other at the corners. The roof was supported by a ridge pole, resting on two upright forks of about ten feet high; that and the wall support a certain number of spars, which are covered with spruce bark; and the whole attached and secured by the fibers of the cedar. One of the gable ends is closed with split boards; the other with poles. Large rods are also fixed across the upper part of the building, where fish may hang and dry. To give the walls additional strength, upright posts are fixed in the ground, at equal distances, both within and without, of the same height as the wall, and firmly attached with bark fibres.

===Chinese===

许多云杉的树皮已被剥走，我推测是被土著拿去盖他们的棚屋了。我们把鹿肉搬上船，继续航行，西南一英里，正南一英里半，正西一英里。到这里，乡野变了模样；河岸只有中等高度，地面从这里渐渐向内隆起，延伸到很远处，长满杨树和柏树，却没有任何下木。还有几处低岸，此处宽约三百码的河有时会漫过它们，那里生长着白杨、软桦、云杉和柳树，绿荫覆岸。在到达这段河道之前的一段路上，我们的视野一直被崎岖、参差而高峻的河岸局限着，岸上杂生着杨树、各种云杉、小桦树、雪松、桤木和几种柳树。我们的下一段航向是西南偏西六英里，随后我们在一座废弃的屋子旁登岸——这是我在米奇利麦基诺（Michilimackinac）以西见到的唯一一座这种样式的印第安居所。它约三十英尺长、二十英尺宽，有三扇门，各高三英尺、宽一英尺半。从这和其他迹象看，它是为三户人家盖的。屋里还有三个火塘，彼此等距；床位在火塘两侧。床后面有一条窄窄的空间，形如马槽，略微垫高，专门用来存鱼。屋子五英尺高的墙壁由笔直的云杉木料构成，彼此贴紧，四角相互咬合。屋顶由一根脊橺承起，脊橺架在两根约十英尺高的立叉上；脊橺和墙壁共同支撑起一定数目的椽条，上面覆盖云杉树皮；整体用雪松的纤维缚牢加固。一端的山墙用劈板封死，另一端用木杆。建筑物的上部还横固定着大木杆，可以挂鱼晾干。为增加墙的牢固，内外两侧都等距埋设立柱，与墙同高，用树皮纤维牢牢缚住。

===Original===

Openings appear also between the logs in the wall, for the purpose, as I conjectured, of discharging their arrows at a besieging enemy; they would be needless for the purpose of giving light, which is sufficiently afforded by fissures between the logs of the building, so that it appeared to be constructed merely for a summer habitation. There was nothing further to attract our attention in or about the house, except a large machine, which must have rendered the taking off the roof absolutely necessary, in order to have introduced it. It was of a cylindrical form, fifteen feet long, and four feet and an half in diameter; one end was square, like the head of a cask, and a conical machine was fixed inwards to the other end, of similar dimensions; at the extremity of which was an opening of about seven inches in diameter. This machine was certainly contrived to set in the river, to catch large fish; and very well adapted to that purpose; as when they are once in, it must be impossible for them to get out, unless they should have strength sufficient to break through it. It was made of long pieces of split wood, rounded to the size of a small finger, and placed at the distance of an inch asunder, on six hoops; to this was added a kind of boot of the same materials, into which it may be supposed that the fish are driven, when they are to be taken out. The house was left in such apparent order as to mark the design of its owners to return thither. It answered in every particular the description given us by our late guide, except that it was not situated on an island.
We left this place, and steered south by east one mile and a quarter when we passed where there had been another house, of which the ridgepole and supporters alone remained: the ice had probably carried away the body of it. The bank was at this time covered with water, and a small river flowed in on the left. On a point we observed an erection that had the appearance of a tomb; it was in an oblong form, covered, and very neatly walled with bark. A pole was fixed near it, to which, at the height of ten or twelve feet, a piece of bark was attached, which was probably a memorial, or symbol of distinction. Our next course was south by west two miles and a half, when we saw a house on an island, southeast by east one mile and three quarters, in which we observed another island, with a house upon it.

===Chinese===

墙上的圆木之间还留有一些开口，据我推测，是用来向围攻的敌人放箭的；若为采光，这些孔便属多余，因为建筑木料间的缝隙透进的光已足够用，可见这屋子只是为夏季居住而建的。屋里屋外再没有什么足以引起我们注意的东西，只有一件大机器——要把它搬进屋，必定得先拆掉屋顶不可。它呈圆筒形，长十五英尺，直径四英尺半；一端呈方形，像桶头，另一端向内安着一个同样尺寸的锥形装置；锥顶有一个直径约七英寸的开口。这件装置显然是为了安置在河里捕捉大鱼而设计的，也非常合乎这一用途；鱼一旦进去，除非有力气撞破它，绝无逃脱的可能。它由长长的劈木条制成，木条削成小指粗细，间隔一英寸，箍在六道箍圈上；末端还添了一个同样材料做的“靴筒”，可以想见，取鱼时鱼便被赶到那里面。这屋子被弃置得井然有序，看得出主人有意回来。它与先前的向导向我们描述的处处吻合，只是并非坐落在岛上。
我们离开此地，向南偏东行一英里又四分之一，经过一处曾有另一座屋子的地方，如今只剩下脊橺和支柱：屋身多半是被冰凌卷走的。此时岸边已被水淹没，一条小河从左边汇入。在一处地角上，我们看到一座形似坟墓的建筑；它呈长方形，有覆盖，四壁用树皮砌得非常齐整。旁边立着一根杆子，在十到十二英尺高处系着一块树皮，多半是一种纪念物或尊荣的标志。我们的下一段航向是南偏西二英里半，随后望见一座岛上有座屋子；再东南偏东一英里又四分之三，其间又见一座岛，上面也有座屋子。

===Original===

A river also flowed from the right, and the land was high and rocky, and wooded with the épinette.
Our canoe was now become so crazy that it was a matter of absolute necessity to construct another; and as from the appearance of the country there was reason to expect that bark was to be found, we landed at eight, with the hope of procuring it. I accordingly dispatched four men with that commission, and at twelve they returned with a sufficient quantity to make the bottom of a canoe of five fathom in length, and four feet and a half in height. At noon I had an observation, which gave me 53° 17′ 28″ north latitude.
We now continued our voyage southeast by south one mile and a half, east-southeast one mile, east-northeast half a mile, southeast two miles, southeast by south one mile, southeast six miles, and east-northeast. Here the river narrows between steep rocks, and a rapid succeeded, which was so violent that we did not venture to run it. I therefore ordered the loading to be taken out of the canoe, but she was now become so heavy that the men preferred running the rapid to the carrying her overland. Though I did not altogether approve of their proposition, I was unwilling to oppose it. Four of them undertook this hazardous expedition, and I hastened to the foot of the rapid with great anxiety, to wait the event, which turned out as I expected. The water was so strong, that although they kept clear of the rocks, the canoe filled, and in this state they drove halfway down the rapid, but fortunately she did not overset; and having got her into an eddy, they emptied her, and in an half-drowned condition arrived safe on shore. The carrying-place is about half a mile over, with an Indian path across it. Mr. Mackay, and the hunters, saw some deer on an island above the rapid; and had that discovery been made before the departure of the canoe, there is little doubt but we should have added a considerable quantity of venison to our stock of provisions. Our vessel was in such a wretched condition, as I have already observed, that it occasioned a delay of three hours to put her in a condition to proceed.

===Chinese===

一条河也从右边流来，这一带土地高峻多岩，长满了云杉。
我们的独木舟已经破败到非再造一只不可的地步；从这一带的地貌看，有理由指望找到树皮，于是我们八点登岸，希望弄到它。我随即派了四个人去办这件差事，十二点他们回来了，带回的数量足够做一只五英寻长、四英尺半高的独木舟的船底。正午我作了一次观测，得北纬53°17′28″。
我们随即继续航行，东南偏南一英里半，东南东一英里，东东北半英里，东南二英里，东南偏南一英里，东南六英里，再东东北。这里河道在陡峭的岩石间收窄，接着是一道激流，水势凶猛，我们不敢冲下去。我于是下令卸下独木舟的载货，但它此刻已沉重得让人们宁可冲激流也不愿从陆地上抬它。我虽不十分赞成他们的提议，却也不愿反对。四个人承担了这趟冒险，我焦灼地赶到激流脚下等候结果，结果不出我所料。水势太猛，他们虽然避开了岩石，独木舟还是灌满了水，就这样灌着水冲下了半程激流，所幸没有翻；把舟弄进一处回水湾排空水后，他们浑身半湿地安全靠了岸。搬运点约半英里，中间有一条印第安小径。麦凯先生和猎手们在激流上方的一座岛上看见几只鹿；倘若这一发现早于独木舟出发，毫无疑问我们的口粮本可以添上一大批鹿肉。如前所述，我们的船已破败不堪，把它修到能继续前行，耽搁了三个小时。

===Original===

At length we continued our former course, east-northeast a mile and a half, when we passed an extensive Indian encampment; east-southeast one mile, where a small river appeared on the left; southeast by south one mile and three quarters, east by south half a mile, east by north one mile, and saw another house on an island; south half a mile, west three quarters of a mile, southwest half a mile, where the cliffs of white and red clay appeared like the ruins of ancient castles. Our canoe now veered gradually to east-northeast one mile and a half, when we landed in a storm of rain and thunder, where we perceived the remains of Indian houses. It was impossible to determine the wind in any part of the day, as it came ahead in all our directions.
*Friday, June 21.*﻿—As I was very sensible of the difficulty of procuring provisions in this country, I thought it prudent to guard against any possibility of distress of that kind on our return; I therefore ordered ninety pounds weight of pemmican to be buried in a hole, sufficiently deep to admit of a fire over it without doing any injury to our hidden treasure, and which would, at the same time, secure it from the natives of the country, or the wild animals of the woods.
The morning was very cloudy, and at four o’clock we renewed our voyage, steering south by east one mile and a quarter, east-southeast half a mile, south by east one mile and a half, east half a mile, southeast two miles, where a large river flowed in from the left, and a smaller one from the right. We then continued south by west three quarters of a mile, east by south a mile and a half, south three quarters of a mile, southeast by east one mile, south by east half a mile, southeast three quarters of a mile, southeast by south half a mile, southeast by east half a mile, the cliffs of blue and yellow clay, displaying the same grotesque shapes as those which we passed yesterday, south-southeast a mile and a half, south by east two miles. The latitude by observation was 52° 47′ 51″ north.
Here we perceived a small new canoe, that had been drawn up to the edge of the woods, and soon after another appeared, with one man in it, which came out of a small river. He no sooner saw us than he gave the whoop to alarm his friends, who immediately appeared on the bank, armed with bows and arrows, and spears.

===Chinese===

我们终于继续原先的航向，东东北一英里半，经过一大片印第安营地；东南东一英里，一条小河出现在左边；东南偏南一英里又四分之三，东偏南半英里，东偏北一英里，又望见一座岛上有座屋子；正南半英里，正西四分之三英里，西南半英里，这里白垩和红土的峭壁看去宛如古城堡的废墟。独木舟随即渐渐折向东东北一英里半，我们在一阵雷雨中登岸，见到印第安屋舍的残迹。这一整天都无法判定风向，因为不管我们朝哪个方向走，风总是顶着我们。
*6月21日，星期五*——我深知在这一带弄到口粮有多难，便认为必须未雨绸缪，防止归程中发生那种窘困；于是我命人把九十磅干肉饼（pemmican）埋进一个坑里，坑深得足以在上面生火而不伤及我们这份秘藏，同时又能使它免遭当地土著或林中野兽的祸害。
早晨多云，四点钟我们重新上路，朝南偏东一英里又四分之一，东南东半英里，南偏东一英里半，正东半英里，东南二英里，一条大河从左边汇入，一条小些的从右边汇入。我们随后继续南偏西四分之三英里，东偏南一英里半，正南四分之三英里，东南偏东一英里，南偏东半英里，东南四分之三英里，东南偏南半英里，东南偏东半英里，蓝垩和黄土的峭壁显出同我们昨天经过的那些一样怪异的形状；再南南东一英里半，南偏东二英里。观测所得纬度为北纬52°47′51″。
在这里，我们发现一只崭新的小独木舟被拖到了林边，不久又出现一只，里面坐着一个人，从一条小河里划出来。他一见我们便发出警报的呼喊，他的同伴们立刻出现在岸上，张弓搭箭，持着长矛。

===Original===

They were thinly habited, and displayed the most outrageous antics. Though they were certainly in a state of great apprehension, they manifested by their gestures that they were resolved to attack us, if we should venture to land. I therefore ordered the men to stop the way of the canoe, and even to check her drifting with the current, as it would have been extreme folly to have approached these savages before their fury had in some degree subsided. My interpreters, who understood their language, informed me that they threatened us with instant death if we drew nigh the shore; and they followed the menace by discharging a volley of arrows, some of which fell short of the canoe, and others passed over it, so that they fortunately did us no injury.
As we had been carried by the current below the spot where the Indians were, I ordered my people to paddle to the opposite side of the river, without the least appearance of confusion, so that they brought me abreast of them. My interpreters, while we were within hearing, had done everything in their power to pacify them, but in vain. We also observed that they had sent off a canoe with two men, down the river, as we concluded, to communicate their alarm, and procure assistance. This circumstance determined me to leave no means untried that might engage us in a friendly intercourse with them, before they acquired additional security and confidence, by the arrival of their relations and neighbours, to whom their situation would be shortly notified.
I therefore formed the following adventurous project, which was happily crowned with success. I left the canoe, and walked by myself along the beach, in order to induce some of the natives to come to me, which I imagined they might be disposed to do, when they saw me alone, without any apparent possibility of receiving assistance from my people, and would consequently imagine that a communication with me was not a service of danger.

===Chinese===

他们衣着单薄，做出最狂乱的怪态。虽然他们肯定处于极大的惊恐之中，却以手势表示：倘若我们胆敢登岸，他们决意向我们进攻。我于是命人停住独木舟的去势，甚至顶住水流的漂送——在这些野人的怒气稍稍平息之前就凑近他们，未免太过愚蠢。我的翻译们听得懂他们的话，告诉我：如果我们靠近河岸，他们便扬言要我们立刻死；威胁过后便射来一阵箭，有些落在独木舟前，有些从上面飞过，幸而没有伤着我们。
由于我们已被水流带到印第安人所在处的下游，我命我的人把独木舟划到河对面去，丝毫不显慌乱，划到与他们并排的位置。在他们听得见的地方，我的翻译们已竭尽所能安抚他们，但徒劳无功。我们还注意到，他们已放出一只载着两人的独木舟顺流而下——我们推断，是去传递警报、搬取援兵的。这一情况使我下定决心，趁他们的亲属邻里尚未赶到——他们的处境很快便会通报过去，使他们凭添依靠和胆气——不惜一切手段同他们缔结友好交往。
于是我拟定了下面这个冒险的计划，它幸运地大获成功。我离开独木舟，独自沿着河滩走去，想引诱几个土著到我这里来；我想，当他们见我孤身一人、显然无法从部下那里得到接应时，或许会愿意过来，并因此觉得同我打交道并非一桩危险的差事。

===Original===

At the same time, in order to possess the utmost security of which my situation was susceptible, I directed one of the Indians to slip into the woods, with my gun and his own, and to conceal himself from their discovery; he also had orders to keep as near me as possible, without being seen; and if any of the natives should venture across, and attempt to shoot me from the water, it was his instructions to lay him low: at the same time he was particularly enjoined not to fire till I had discharged one or both of the pistols that I carried in my belt. If, however, any of them were to land, and approach my person, he was immediately to join me. In the meantime my other interpreter assured them that we entertained the most friendly dispositions, which I confirmed by such signals as I conceived would be comprehended by them. I had not, indeed, been long at my station, and my Indian in ambush behind me, when two of the natives came off in a canoe, but stopped when they had got within a hundred yards of me. I made signs for them to land, and as an inducement, displayed looking-glasses, beads, and other alluring trinkets. At length, but with every mark of extreme apprehension, they approached the shore, stern foremost, but would not venture to land. I now made them a present of some beads, with which they were going to push off, when I renewed my entreaties, and, after some time, prevailed on them to come ashore, and sit down by me. My hunter now thought it right to join me, and created some alarm in my new acquaintance. It was, however, soon removed, and I had the satisfaction to find, that he and these people perfectly understood each other. I instructed him to say everything that might tend to soothe their fears and win their confidence. I expressed my wish to conduct them to our canoe, but they declined my offer; and when they observed some of my people coming towards us, they requested me to let them return; and I was so well satisfied with the progress I had made in my intercourse with them, that I did not hesitate a moment in complying with their desire. During their short stay, they observed us, and everything about us, with a mixture of admiration and astonishment.

===Chinese===

与此同时，为了让我处境中的安全达到极致，我指派一名印第安人带着我的枪和他自己的枪悄悄潜入林中，躲开他们的视线；他还奉命在不被发现的前提下尽量靠近我；倘若有土著敢渡河而来、企图从水上射我，他的指令便是将那人击倒；同时特别叮嘱他，非等我先开枪——我腰带上别着一对手枪——一支或两支都打响，他不得开火。但若有人登岸逼近我身边，他须立即与我汇合。这期间，我的另一名翻译向他们保证我们怀有最友好的心意，我又用自忖他们能看懂的手势加以确认。我在位置上站了不久，我的印第安人也已在背后埋伏妥当，两个土著便乘独木舟过来，但到距我一百码处便停住了。我招手让他们靠岸，为引诱他们，还展示了镜子、珠子和其他诱人的小玩意。终于，他们带着极度惊恐的神情接近岸边——船尾朝前——却不肯登岸。我随即送了他们一些珠子，他们收了正要划走，我又再三恳求，过了一阵总算说动他们上岸，在我身边坐下。这时我的猎手认为该过来同我汇合，一时惊动了我这两位新相识；不过惊慌很快消除，而且我满意地发现，他同这些人完全能彼此听懂。我让他把足以平息恐惧、赢得信任的话统统说给他们。我表示想领他们到我们的独木舟那里去，他们谢绝了；而当他们看见我的几个人朝我们走来时，便请求让他们回去；我对自己同他们交往的进展已很满意，便毫不迟疑地答应了他们的愿望。在他们逗留的短短时间里，他们带着又惊叹又诧异的神情打量着我们和我们周围的一切。

===Original===

We could plainly distinguish that their friends received them with great joy on their return, and that the articles which they carried back with them were examined with a general and eager curiosity; they also appeared to hold a consultation, which lasted about a quarter of an hour, and the result was, an invitation to come over to them, which was cheerfully accepted. Nevertheless, on our landing they betrayed evident signs of confusion, which arose probably from the quickness of our movements, as the prospect of a friendly communication had so cheered the spirits of my people, that they paddled across the river with the utmost expedition. The two men, however, who had been with us, appeared, very naturally, to possess the greatest share of courage on the occasion, and were ready to receive us on our landing; but our demeanour soon dispelled all their apprehensions, and the most familiar communication took place between us. When I had secured their confidence, by the distribution of trinkets among them, and treated the children with sugar, I instructed my interpreters to collect every necessary information in their power to afford me.
According to their account, this river, whose course is very extensive, runs towards the midday sun; and that at its mouth, as they had been informed, white people were building houses. They represented its current to be uniformly strong, and that in three places it was altogether impassable, from the falls and rapids, which poured along between perpendicular rocks that were much higher, and more rugged, than any we had yet seen, and would not admit of any passage over them. But besides the dangers and difficulties of the navigation, they added, that we should have to encounter the inhabitants of the country, who were very numerous. They also represented their immediate neighbours as a very malignant race, who lived in large subterraneous recesses; and when they were made to understand that it was our design to proceed to the sea, they dissuaded us from prosecuting our intention, as we should certainly become a sacrifice to the savage spirit of the natives. These people they described as possessing iron, arms, and utensils, which they procured from their neighbours to the westward, and were obtained by a commercial progress from people like ourselves, who brought them in great canoes.

===Chinese===

我们可以清楚地看出，他们的同伴在他们回去时欢天喜地地迎接，他们带回的物件被众人怀着热切的好奇一一察看；他们似乎还开了一场协商，持续约一刻钟，结果是发出邀请，请我们到他们那边去，我们欣然接受。然而在我们登岸时，他们还是流露出明显的慌乱，这多半是由于我们行动太快——友好交往的前景使我的人精神大振，他们以最快的速度划过了河。不过，同我们相处过的那两人，此时自然表现出最大的胆气，准备在岸边迎接我们；我们的举止很快打消了他们一切的疑惧，双方便展开了最随便不过的交谈。我用分发小玩意赢得了他们的信任，又拿糖款待了孩子们，随后便吩咐我的翻译们把他们所能提供的一切必要信息都收集起来。
据他们讲，这条河流程极长，朝着正午太阳的方向流去；据他们所闻，在河口，白人正在盖房子。他们说这条河水流处处湍急，有三处因瀑布和激流全然无法通行——水在陡直的岩壁间倾泻而下，那些岩壁比我们迄今所见的都更高、更险峻，根本无从翻越。除航行的艰险之外，他们还说，我们将不得不对付这一带的居民，他们为数众多。他们还把自己紧邻的那族人描绘得非常凶恶，住在巨大的地窟里；而当他们明白我们的打算是一路走到海边时，便劝我们打消这个念头，说我们必沦为土著凶野之气的祭品。据他们描述，这些人拥有铁器、武器和器皿，是从他们西边的邻居那里得来的，而那些人又是通过层层贸易从像我们一样的人手里获得的——那些人乘着大独木舟把货物运来。

===Original===

Such an account of our situation, exaggerated as it might be in some points, and erroneous in others, was sufficiently alarming, and awakened very painful reflections: nevertheless it did not operate on my mind so as to produce any change in my original determination. My first object, therefore, was to persuade two of these people to accompany me, that they might secure to us a favourable reception from their neighbours. To this proposition they assented, but expressed some degree of dissatisfaction at the immediate departure, for which we were making preparation; but when we were ready to enter the canoe, a small one was seen doubling the point below, with three men in it. We thought it prudent to wait for their arrival, and they proved to be some of their relations, who had received the alarm from the messengers which I have already mentioned as having been sent down the river for that purpose, and who had passed on, as we were afterwards informed, to extend the notice of our arrival. Though these people saw us in the midst of their friends, they displayed the most menacing actions, and hostile postures. At length, however, this wild, savage spirit appeared to subside, and they were persuaded to land. One of them, who was a middle aged person, whose agitations had been less frequent than those of his companions, and who was treated with particular respect by them all, inquired who we were, whence we came, whither we were going, and what was the motive of our coming into that country. When his friends had satisfied him as far as they were able, respecting us, he instantly advised us to delay our departure for that night, as their relations below, having been by this time alarmed by the messengers, who had been sent for that purpose, would certainly oppose our passage, notwithstanding I had two of their own people with me. He added, that they would all of them be here by sunset, they would convinced, as he was, that we were good people, and meditated no ill designs against them.
Such were the reasons which this Indian urged in favour of our remaining till the next morning; and they were too well founded for me to hesitate in complying with them; besides, by prolonging my stay till the next morning, it was probable that I might obtain some important intelligence respecting the country through which I was to pass, and the people who inhabited it.

===Chinese===

这样一番关于我们处境的陈述，尽管某些地方或有夸大，某些地方或有说误，已足够令人心惊，唤起了非常痛苦的思虑；但它并没有动摇我的心，使我改变最初的决定。因此我的第一个目标，便是说动这些人中的两个随我同行，好让他们的邻人给我们一个友善的接待。他们答应了这一提议，但对马上动身表示了某种不满——我们正在做出发的准备；可就在我们准备上船时，只见下方地角处转出一只小独木舟，载着三个人。我们认为等他们到来才是明智之举；他们果然是这些人的亲戚，已从先前我提到过的、为传信而派往下游的使者们那里得到了警报；据我们后来所知，那些使者已继续前行，把我们到来的消息传得更远。这些人虽看见我们身处他们的亲友中间，仍旧做出最凶恶的动作和敌对的姿态。不过，这股狂野的蛮气终于平息下去，他们被劝得登了岸。其中一个是中年人，激动得比同伴们少，又受众人特别的敬重，他问我们是谁、从哪里来、到哪里去、到这个国家来有何缘由。亲友们就其所知向他说明之后，他立即建议我们把行期推迟到当夜之后，因为下游的亲属此刻已从专程报信的使者那里得了警报，必定会阻拦我们的去路，哪怕我带着他们的两个族人。他还说，他们日落之前都会到这儿来，到时他们也会像他一样确信我们是好人，对他们不怀恶意。
这位印第安人为劝我们留到次日早晨所陈述的理由便是这些；这些理由太有根据了，我不由得依从；何况把停留拖到明晨，我或许还能打听到一些有关我将穿越的乡野及其居民的重要情况。

===Original===

I accordingly ordered the canoe to be unloaded, taken out of the water, and gummed. My tent was also pitched, and the natives were now become so familiar, that I was obliged to let them know my wish to be alone and undisturbed.
My first application to the native whom I have already particularly mentioned, was to obtain from him such a plan of the river as he should be enabled to give me; and he complied with this request with a degree of readiness and intelligence that evidently proved it was by no means a new business to him. In order to acquire the best information he could communicate, I assured him, if I found his account correct, that I should either return myself, or send others to them, with such articles as they appeared to want: particularly arms and ammunition, with which they would be able to prevent their enemies from invading them. I obtained, however, no addition to what I already knew, but that the country below us, as far as he was acquainted with it, abounded in animals, and that the river produced plenty of fish.
Our canoe was now become so weak, leaky, and unmanageable, that it became a matter of absolute necessity to construct a new one; and I had been informed, that if we delayed that important work till we got further down the river, we should not be able to procure bark. I therefore dispatched two of my people, with an Indian, in search of that necessary material. The weather was so cloudy that I could not get an observation.15
I passed the rest of the day in conversing with these people: they consisted of seven families, containing eighteen men, they were clad in leather, and handsome beaver and rabbit-skin blankets. They had not been long arrived in this part of the country, where they proposed to pass the summer, to catch fish for their winter provision: for this purpose they were preparing machines similar to that which we found in the first Indian house we saw and described. The fish which they take in them are large, and only visit this part of the river at certain seasons. These people differ very little, if at all, either in their appearance, language, or manners, from the Rocky Mountain Indians. The men whom I sent in search of bark, returned with a certain quantity of it, but of a very indifferent kind. We were not gratified with the arrival of any of the natives whom we expected from a lower part of the river.

===Chinese===

我于是下令卸空独木舟，把它抬出水，上胶。我的帐篷也支了起来；土著们此时已熟不拘礼，我只得让他们知道，我希望独自待着，不受打扰。
我对那位已特别提及的土著的头一项请求，便是请他把他所能绘出的河道图给我；他应承这请求时的爽利和灵慧，显然证明这对他绝非头一回。为了让他把所知尽数告诉我，我向他保证：倘若发现他说的属实，我或者亲自回来，或者派别人到他们这里来，带上他们看来需要的物件：尤其是枪支弹药，有了这些，他们便能阻止敌人来犯。然而除已知的情况外，我只多得两条：我们下方这一带地方——就他所知——动物很多，河里鱼产丰富。
我们的独木舟此刻已破漏不堪、难以操纵，非造一只新的不可；而我被告知，这项要紧的工程若拖到再往下游去才办，便弄不到树皮了。于是我派了两个人带一名印第安人去寻找这种必需的材料。天阴得厉害，我无法作观测。15
这天余下的时间，我都用来同这些人交谈：他们共七户，十八个男人，身穿皮革，披着漂亮的海狸皮和兔皮毯子。他们到这一带不久，打算在此过夏，捕鱼备作冬季的口粮：为此他们正在制作那种机器，同我们在第一座印第安屋子里见到并描述过的相似。他们在里面捕的鱼个头很大，只在某些季节才到河的这一段来。这些人在相貌、语言或习俗上，同落基山的山地印第安人几乎没有差别。我派去找树皮的人回来了，带回了一些，但质地甚为平平。我们期待的、从河下游来的土著，一个也没有到。

### VIII / 八
===Original===

*Saturday, June 22.*﻿—At six in the morning we proceeded on our voyage, with two of the Indians, one of them in a small pointed canoe, made after the fashion of the Eskimo, and the other in our own. This precaution was necessary in a twofold point of view, as the small canoe could be sent ahead to speak to any of the natives that might be seen down the river, and, thus divided, would not be easy for them both to make their escape. Mr. Mackay also embarked with the Indian, which seemed to afford him great satisfaction, and he was thereby enabled to keep us company with diminution of labour.
Our courses were south-southeast a mile and a half, southeast half a mile, south by east four miles and a half, southeast by south half a mile, south by west half a mile, southeast by east one mile, south-southwest a mile and a half, south by east one mile and a quarter. The country, on the right, presented a very beautiful appearance: it rose at first rather abruptly to the height of twenty-five feet, when the precipice was succeeded by an inclined plain to the foot of another steep; which was followed by another extent of gently-rising ground: these objects, which were shaded with groves of fir, presenting themselves alternately to a considerable distance.
We now landed near a house, the roof of which alone appeared above ground; but it was deserted by its inhabitants who had been alarmed at our approach. We observed several men in the second steep, who displayed the same postures and menacing actions as those which we have so lately described. Our conductors went to them immediately on an embassy of friendship, and, after a very vociferous discourse, one of them was persuaded to come to us, but presented a very ferocious aspect: the rest, who were seven in number, soon followed his example. They held their bows and arrows in their hands, and appeared in their garments, which were fastened round the neck, but left the right arm free for action. A cord fastened a blanket or leather covering under the right armpit, so that it hung upon the left shoulder, and might be occasionally employed as a target, that would turn an arrow which was nearly spent. As soon as they had recovered from their apprehensions, ten women made their appearance, but without any children, whom, I imagine, they had sent to a greater distance, to be out of reach of all possible danger.

===Chinese===

*6月22日，星期六*——早晨六点，我们带着两名印第安人继续航行，一个乘着一只爱斯基摩样式的小尖头独木舟，另一个乘我们自己的。这一预防措施从两方面看都属必要：小独木舟可以派到前面去，同下游可能出现的任何土著搭话；而且这样分开来，他们俩便不易双双逃走。麦凯先生也同那名印第安人一道上了那条小舟，这似乎令他大为满意，他也可以省些力气跟着我们走了。
我们的航向为南南东一英里半，东南半英里，南偏东四英里半，东南偏南半英里，南偏西半英里，东南偏东一英里，南南西一英里半，南偏东一英里又四分之一。右岸的乡野呈现出非常美丽的景致：起初相当陡然地拔起二十五英尺，峭壁之上接一片斜坡平原，通到另一道陡壁脚下；其后又是一片缓缓升起的地面；这些景物覆着一片片冷杉林，交替呈现，绵延很远。
我们随即在一座屋子附近登岸，只有屋顶露在地面之上；但居民已被我们的到来惊走。我们看见第二道陡坡上有几个男人，摆出同我们方才描述过的那些人一样的姿态和威胁动作。我们的向导立即前去进行友好使节式的沟通，经过一场高声的交涉，其中一个被劝说来到我们这里，但面相十分凶悍；其余七人很快也跟着过来。他们手里拿着弓箭，衣裳在颈间系住，右臂袒露以便行动。一根绳索把毯子或皮革在右腋下束住，使它垂在左肩上，必要时可以权当盾牌，挡得住将近力竭的箭。他们惊魂稍定之后，出现了十个女人，却不带一个孩子——我想，孩子们已被送到更远处，以免遭一切可能的危险。

===Original===

I distributed a few presents among them, and left my guides to explain to them the object of my journey, and the friendliness of my designs, with which they had themselves been made acquainted; their fears being at length removed, I gave them a specimen of the use to which we applied our firearms: at the same time, I calmed their astonishment, by the assurance, that, though we could at once destroy those who did us injury, we could equally protect those who showed us kindness. Our stay here did not exceed half an hour, and we left these people with favourable impressions of us.
From this place we steered east by north half a mile, south by east three quarters of a mile, and south by west a mile and a half, when we landed again on seeing some of the natives on the high ground, whose appearance was more wild and ferocious than any whom we had yet seen. Indeed I was under some apprehension that our guides, who went to conciliate them to us, would have fallen a prey to their savage fury. At length, however, they were persuaded to entertain a more favourable opinion of us, and they approached us one after another, to the number of sixteen men, and several women, I shook hands with them all, and desired my interpreters to explain that salutation as a token of friendship. As this was not a place where we could remain with the necessary convenience, I proposed to proceed further, in search of a more commodious spot. They immediately invited us to pass the night at their lodges, which were at no great distance, and promised, at the same time, that they would, in the morning, send two young men to introduce us to the next nation, who were very numerous, and ill-disposed towards strangers. As we were pushing from the shore, we were very much surprised at hearing a woman pronounce several words in the Knisteneaux language. She proved to be a Rocky Mountain native, so that my interpreters perfectly understood her. She informed us that her country is at the forks of this river, and that she had been taken prisoner by the Knisteneaux, who had carried her across the mountains.

===Chinese===

我在他们中间分发了一些礼物，让我的向导们向他们说明此行的目的和我的友好意图——这些向导们自己早已明白；他们的恐惧终于消除后，我向他们演示了我们的火器是做什么用的；同时我平息他们的惊愕，向他们保证：我们固然能一举消灭伤害我们的人，也同样能保护善待我们的人。我们在此停留不过半小时，离开时给这些人留下了对我们的好印象。
从此地我们朝东偏北行半英里，南偏东四分之三英里，南偏西一英里半，随后又登岸，因为望见高地上有一些土著，其相貌比我们迄今所见的任何人都更野蛮凶悍。说实话，我一度担心前去笼络他们的向导会沦为这些人凶野怒气的牺牲品。不过他们终于被说动，对我们有了较好的看法，一个接一个地走近我们，共有十六个男人和几个女人。我同他们一一握手，并让翻译们解释这礼节是友情的凭证。这里不是我们能方便停留的地方，我便提议再往前走，找个更合适的地点。他们当即邀请我们到他们的棚屋去过夜，说那距此不远，并许诺明早派两个年轻人把我们引见给下一个部族——那些人为数众多，且对外来人不怀好意。我们正撜船离岸，忽听一个女人用克里斯提诺人（Knisteneaux）的话说了几句，都不胜惊讶。她原来是个落基山的土人，我的翻译们完全听得懂她。她告诉我们，她的家乡就在这条河的岔口一带，她被克里斯提诺人掳去，被带过了群山。

===Original===

After having passed the greatest part of the summer with them, she had contrived to escape, before they had reached their own country, and had re-crossed the mountains, when she expected to meet her own friends: but after suffering all the hardships incident to such a journey, she had been taken by a war-party of the people with whom she then was, who had driven her relations from the river into the mountains. She had since been detained by her present husband, of whom she had no cause to complain; nevertheless she expressed a strong desire to return to her own people. I presented her with several useful articles, and desired her to come to me at the lodges, which she readily engaged to do. We arrived thither before the Indians, and landed, as we had promised. It was now near twelve at noon, but on attempting to take an altitude, I found the angle too great for my sextant.
The natives whom we had already seen, and several others, soon joined us, with a greater number of women than I had yet seen; but I did not observe the female prisoner among them. There were thirty-five of them, and my remaining store of presents was not sufficient to enable me to be very liberal to so many claimants. Among the men I found four of the adjoining nation, and a Rocky Mountain Indian, who had been with them for some time. As he was understood by my interpreters, and was himself well acquainted with the language of the strangers, I possessed the means of obtaining every information respecting the country, which it might be in their power to afford me. For this purpose I selected an elderly man, from the four strangers, whose countenance had prepossessed me in his favour. I stated to these people, as I had already done to those from whom I had hitherto derived information, the objects of my voyage, and the very great advantages which they would receive from my successful termination of it. They expressed themselves very much satisfied at my communication, and assured me that they would not deceive me respecting the subject of my inquiry. An old man also, who appeared to possess the character of a chief, declared his wish to see me return to his land, and that his two young daughters should then be at my disposal.

===Chinese===

她同他们在那边过了大半个夏天，趁他们还没回到自己的地界，设法逃了出来，重新翻过群山，指望见到自己的亲友；可是在吃尽这样一趟旅程的种种苦头之后，她又被眼下同住的这伙人的一个战斗队掳获——那伙人把她的亲属从河边赶进了山里。此后她便被现今的丈夫扣留下来，倒是没有什么可抱怨的；不过她表示极想回到自己人那里去。我送了她几样有用的东西，请她到棚屋那边来找我，她欣然应允。我们先于印第安人到了那里，如约登岸。此时已近正午十二点，但一测高度，发现角度超出了我的六分仪的量程。
我们先前见过的那些土著和另外几个很快同我们会合，随行的女人比我迄今见过的都多；但我没有见到那个女俘。他们共三十五人，我剩下的礼物存货，不足以让我对这么多索要者个个出手阔绰。在男人中间，我发现了四个邻族的人和一名落基山印第安人，后者已同他们相处了一段时间。由于我的翻译听得懂他，而他本人又熟谙这些陌生人的语言，我便有了手段，可以把这些人所能提供的关于这一带的情况统统弄到手。为此我从四个外人中挑了一位老者，他的面相先就博得了我的好感。我向这些人陈述了——如同向先前提供情报的那些人一样——我此航的目的，以及此行成功将给他们带来的极大好处。他们听了我的话非常满意，向我保证，在我探询的事情上绝不欺骗我。还有一个像是首领人物的老者表示，愿意见我回到他的土地上，还说届时他的两个小女儿任凭我支配。

===Original===

I now proceeded to request the native, whom I had particularly selected, to commence his information, by drawing a sketch of the country upon a large piece of bark, and he immediately entered on the work, frequently appealing to, and sometimes asking the advice of, those around him. He described the river as running to the east of south, receiving many rivers, and every six or eight leagues encumbered with falls and rapids, some of which were very dangerous, and six of them impracticable. The carrying-places he represented as of great length, and passing over hills and mountains. He depicted the lands of three other tribes, in succession, who spoke different languages. Beyond them he knew nothing either of the river or country, only that it was still a long way to the sea; and that, as he had heard, there was a lake, before they reached the water, which the natives did not drink. As far as his knowledge of the river extended, the country on either side was level, in many places without wood and abounding in red deer, and some of a small fallow kind. Few of the natives, he said, would come to the banks for some time; but, that at a certain season they would arrive there in great numbers, to fish. They now procured iron, brass, copper, and trinkets, from the westward; but formerly these articles were obtained from the lower parts of the river, though in small quantities. A knife was produced which had been brought from that quarter. The blade was ten inches long, and an inch and a half broad, but with a very blunted edge. The handle was of horn. We understood that this instrument had been obtained from white men, long before they had heard that any came to the westward. One very old man observed, that as long as he could remember, he was told of white people to the southward; and that he had heard, though he did not vouch for the truth of the report, that one of them had made an attempt to come up the river, and was destroyed.
These people describe the distance across the country as very short to the western ocean; and, according to my own idea, it cannot be above five or six degrees. If the assertion of Mr. Mears be correct, it cannot be so far, as the inland sea which he mentions within Nootka, must come as far east as 126° west longitude.

===Chinese===

我于是请那位特别选中的土人开始提供情况，先在一大张树皮上把这一带画个草图，他立即动起手来，不时向周围的人求助，有时还征求他们的意见。他把这条河描述为向东南偏南方向流去，沿途接纳许多河流，每隔六到八里格就有一处瀑布和激流，有些非常危险，其中六处无法通行。搬运点据他描述都很长，要翻山越岭。他还依次描绘了三个讲不同语言部族的土地。再往前，这条河和乡野的情形他就一无所知了，只知道离海还很远；而且听说，到达那片水之前要先经过一个湖，土人都不喝那湖里的水。就他所知的这段河而言，两岸地势平坦，许多地方没有树木，赤鹿很多，还有一种小型的黄鹿。他说，一时半会儿没有什么土人会到河边来；但到了某个季节，他们会大批到这里捕鱼。他们如今从西边弄到铁、黄铜、紫铜和小饰品；不过从前这些物件是从河的下游弄来的，尽管数量不多。有人拿出了一把从那一带来的刀：刀刃十英寸长、一英寸半宽，刃口却很钝；柄是角的。我们了解到，这件东西是从白人那里得来的，远在他们听说有白人到西边来之前。一位很老的人说，从他记事起，就听说南边有白人；还听说——虽不敢担保这话属实——有一个白人曾试图沿河上来，被消灭了。
这些人说，横穿这片乡野到西面大洋去的路很近；照我自己的看法，不会超过五六度。倘若米尔斯先生（Mr. Mears）的说法属实，距离还要更近，因为他提到努特卡（Nootka）里面的那个内海，必定向东延伸到西经126°一带。

===Original===

They assured us that the road was not difficult as they avoided the mountains, keeping along the low lands between them, many parts of which are entirely free from wood. According to their account, this way is so often travelled by them, that their path is visible throughout the whole journey, which lies along small lakes and rivers. It occupied them, they said, no more than six nights, to go to where they meet the people who barter iron, brass, copper, beads, etc., with them, for dressed leather, and beaver, bear, lynx, fox, and marten skins. The iron is about eighteen inches of two-inch bar. To this they give an edge at one end, and fix it to a handle at right angles, which they employ as an axe. When the iron is worn down, they fabricate it into points for their arrows and pikes. Before they procured iron they employed bone and horn for those purposes. The copper and brass they convert into collars, arm-buds, bracelets, and other ornaments. They sometimes also point their arrows with those metals. They had been informed by those whom they meet to trade with, that the white people, from whom these articles are obtained, were building houses at the distance of three days, or two nights journey from the place where they met last fall. With this route they all appeared to be well acquainted.
I now requested that they would send for the female prisoner whom I saw yesterday; but I received only vague and evasive answers. They probably apprehended, that it was our design to take her from them. I was, however, very much disappointed at being prevented from having an interview with her, as she might have given me a correct account of the country beyond the forks of the river, as well as of the pass, through the mountains, from them.
My people had listened with great attention to the relation which had been given me, and it seemed to be their opinion, that it would be absolute madness to attempt a passage through so many savage and barbarous nations.

===Chinese===

他们向我们担保这条路不难走，因为他们避开群山，沿着山间的低地走，许多地段完全没有树木。据他们讲，这条路他们常走，小径全程可见，一路沿着小湖和小河延伸。他们说，到那同他们以鞣好的皮革以及海狸、熊、猞猁、狐狸、貂皮交换铁、黄铜、紫铜、珠子等物的人那里，不过花六个夜晚。铁是两英寸宽的铁条，约十八英寸长。他们把一端开出刃口，再安上一个直角的柄，当斧头用。铁磨坏了，便打制成箭头和矛尖。在没有铁之前，这些用途都用兽骨和角。铜和黄铜他们做成项圈、臂章、镯子及其他饰品，有时也用来做箭镞。据同他们交易的人相告，提供这些物件的白人，去年秋天他们相遇的地方再走三天——或两夜——路远的地方正在盖房子。这条路他们个个看来都烂熟。
我随后请他们把昨天见到的那个女俘叫来；但得到的只是含糊闪烁的回答。他们多半是担心我们打算把她带走。未能同她见面使我非常失望，因为她本可以告诉我河岔以下地方的准确情形，也可以告诉我土人翻越群山的那个山口。

===Original===

My situation may indeed, be more easily conceived than expressed: I had no more than thirty days provision remaining, exclusive of such supplies as I might obtain from the natives, and the toil of our hunters, which, however, was so precarious as to be matter of little dependence: besides, our ammunition would soon be exhausted, particularly our ball, of which we had not more than a hundred and fifty, and about thirty pound weight of shot, which, indeed, might be converted into bullets, though with great waste.
The more I heard of the river, the more I was convinced it could not empty itself into the ocean to the north of what is called the river of the west, so that with its windings, the distance must be very great. Such being the discouraging circumstances of my situation, which were now heightened by the discontents of my people, I could not but be alarmed at the idea of attempting to get to the discharge of such a rapid river, especially when I reflected on the tardy progress of my return up it, even if I should meet with no obstruction from the natives; a circumstance not very probable, from the numbers of them which would then be on the river, and whom I could have no opportunity of conciliating in my passage down, for the reasons which have been already mentioned. At all events, I must give up every expectation of returning this season to Athabasca. Such were my reflections at this period; but instead of continuing to indulge them, I determined to proceed with resolution, and set future events at defiance. At the same time I suffered myself to nourish the hope that I might be able to penetrate with more safety, and in a shorter period, to the ocean by the inland western communication.
To carry this project into execution I must have returned a considerable distance up the river, which would necessarily be attended with very serious inconvenience, if I passed over every other; as in a voyage of this kind, a retrograde motion could not fail to cool the ardour, slacken the zeal and weaken the confidence of those, who have no greater inducement to the undertaking, than to follow the conductor of it. Such was the state of my mind at this period, and such the circumstances with which it was distressed and distracted.

===Chinese===

我的人一直非常专注地听着这番讲述，他们的看法似乎是：想穿过这么多凶野蛮荒的部族，简直是发疯。
我的处境实在只能意会而难以言传：口粮只剩三十天的了——这还不算或可从土人那里得到的接济和猎手们的辛劳，但那两样都靠不住，做不得多少指望；何况弹药也快耗尽了，尤其是弹丸，只剩一百五十发不到，另有约三十磅霞弹，霞弹固然可以熔制成弹丸，但耗损极大。
这条河我听得越多，越确信它不会注入所谓“西河”以北的大洋；加上一路曲折，路程必定极远。我的处境既已如此令人气馁，又添上部下的不满，一想到要闯到这样一条湍急大河的河口，我便不能不心惊，尤其想到即便土人毫不阻挠，回程逆流而上也将步履维缓——何况阻挠的可能性不小，届时河上将满是土人，而我顺流而下时根本无暇去笼络他们，原因前文已经说过。无论如何，这个季节返回阿萨巴斯卡（Athabasca）的指望是彻底没有了。这便是我此时的思虑；但我没有沉溺下去，而是决意坚决前行，把未来的事变置之度外。同时我也怀着一线希望：取道西边的内陆水路，或许能更安全、更快地抵达大洋。
要实行这个方案，我必须溯河而上返回相当远的路程，这必然带来非常严重的不便——别的不说，在这样的航行中，走回头路免不了冷却那些人的热忱、松懈他们的干劲、动摇他们的信心，何况他们投身此役，除追随领队之外别无动机。这便是我此时的心境，这便是使它烦扰纷乱的重重情势。

===Original===

To the people who had given me the foregoing information I presented some beads, which they preferred to any other articles in my possession, and I recompensed in the same manner two of them who communicated to me the following vocabulary in the language of the Nagailer and Ahtna tribes.

The Ahtna language has no affinity to any with which I am acquainted; but the Nagailer differs very little from that spoken by the Beaver Indians, and is almost the same as that of the Chipewyans.
We had a thunderstorm with heavy rain; and in the evening when it had subsided, the Indians amused us with singing and dancing, in which they were joined by the young women. Four men now arrived whom we had not yet seen; they had left their families at some distance in the country, and expressed a desire that we should visit them there.
*Sunday, June 23.*﻿—After a restless night, I called the Indians together, from whom I yesterday received the intelligence which has been already mentioned, in the hope that I might obtain some additional information. From their former account they did not make the least deviation; but they informed me further, that where they left this river, a small one from the westward falls into it, which was navigable for their canoes during four days, and from thence they slept but two nights, to get to the people with whom they trade, and who have wooden canoes much larger than ours, in which they go down a river to the sea. They continued to inform me, that if I went that way we must leave our own canoe behind us; but they thought it probable that those people would furnish us with another.

===Chinese===

我向提供上述情况的人赠送了一些珠子——比起我手中的任何其他物件，他们更中意珠子——又以同样的方式酬谢了其中两人，他们向我提供了下面这份纳盖尔（Nagailer）和阿赫特纳（Ahtna）部族语言的词汇表。
眼睛，Nah，Thlouatin。
头发，Thigah，Cahowdin。
牙齿，Gough，Chliough。
鼻子，Nenzeh，Pisax。
头，Thie，Scapacay。
木头，Dekin，Shedzay。
手，Lah，Calietha。
腿，Kin，Squacht。
舌头，Thoula，Dewhasjiak。
耳朵，Zach，Ithlinah。
男人，Dinay，Scuyloch。
女人，Chiquoi，Smosledgenak。
海狸，Zah，Schugh。
麋鹿，Yezey，Ookoy-Beh。
狗，Sleing，Scacah。
旱獭，Thidnu，Squaisquais。
铁，Thilisitch，Soucoumang。
火，Coun，Teuck。
水，Tou，Shaweliquolih。
石头，Zeh，Ishehoinah。
弓，Nettuny，Isquoinah。
箭，Igah，Squailai。
是，Nesi，Amaig。
平原，Thoughoud，Spilela。
过来，Andezei，Thla-elyeh。
阿赫特纳语同我熟悉的任何语言都没有亲缘；但纳盖尔语同海狸印第安人（Beaver Indians）的话相差无几，同奇佩维扬人（Chipewyans）的话几乎一样。
我们遇上了一场雷雨，雨下得很猛；傍晚雨歇后，印第安人唱歌跳舞为我们助兴，年轻的女人们也加入进来。这时又来了四个我们未曾见过的男人；他们把家眷留在内陆远处，表示希望我们到那里去看望他们。
*6月23日，星期日*——一夜无眠之后，我把昨天向我提供上述情况的印第安人召集起来，指望再得一些补充的消息。他们对原先的说法没有丝毫改动；但进一步告诉我们：在他们离开这条河的地方，一条小河从西边汇入，他们的独木舟在那条河上可走四天，从那里再睡两夜，就到同他们交易的人那里；那些人有木制的大独木舟，比我们的大得多，他们乘着那种船顺一条河到海边。他们继续告诉我：倘若我走那条路，必须把我们自己的独木舟留下；但他们想，那些人多半会给我们另备一只。

===Original===

From thence they stated the distance to be only one day’s voyage with the current to the lake whose water is nauseous, and where they had heard that great canoes came two winters ago, and that the people belonging to them, brought great quantities of goods and built houses.
At the commencement of this conversation, I was very much surprised by the following question from one of the Indians: “What,” demanded he, “can be the reason that you are so particular and anxious in your inquiries of us respecting a knowledge of this country: do not you white men know everything in the world?” This interrogatory was so very unexpected, that it occasioned some hesitation before I could answer it. At length, however, I replied, that we certainly were acquainted with the principal circumstances of every part of the world; that I knew where the sea is, and where I myself then was, but that I did not exactly understand what obstacles might interrupt me in getting to it; with which, he and his relations must be well acquainted, as they had so frequently surmounted them. Thus I fortunately preserved the impression in their minds, of the superiority of white people over themselves.
It was now, however, absolutely necessary that I should come to a final determination which route to take; and no long interval of reflection was employed, before I preferred to go over land: the comparative shortness and security of such a journey, were alone sufficient to determine me. I accordingly proposed to two of the Indians to accompany me, and one of them readily assented to my proposition.
I now called those of my people about me, who had not been present at my consultation with the natives; and after passing a warm eulogium on their fortitude, patience, and perseverance, I stated the difficulties that threatened our continuing to navigate the river, the length of time it would require, and the scanty provision we had for such a voyage: I then proceeded for the foregoing reasons to propose a shorter route, by trying the overland road to the sea.

===Chinese===

据他们说，从那里顺流行船一天便到那个湖水令人作呕的湖；听说两个冬天前，大船曾到过那里，船上的人运来了大批货物，还盖了房子。
这场谈话开始时，一个印第安人的一个问题令我大为吃惊：“你们白人，”他问，“对我们这样刨根问底、急于知道这个地方的情形，究竟是什么缘故？你们白人不是天下的事样样都知道吗？”这一问来得太突然，我竟迟疑了一下才答上来。末了我回答说：我们确实熟知世界各地的基本情况；我知道海在哪里，也知道我自己此刻在哪里，只是不能确切弄清一路上会有什么障碍拦住我去海边的路；这些障碍，他和他的亲友必定了如指掌，因为他们曾多次闯过。这样，我幸运地保住了他们心中白人高于自己的印象。
不过，此刻我必须最后决定走哪条路；没用多久斟酌，我便选择了走陆路：这段路比较短、比较稳妥，仅此两条便足以使我下定决心。我于是向两个印第安人提议同行，其中一人欣然应允。
我随即把未参加我同土人商谈的部下叫到跟前，先热烈称赞了他们的坚毅、忍耐与顽强，然后陈述了继续在河上航行的种种困难、所需的长久时日，以及我们口粮的匮乏：接着说明，基于上述理由，我提议改走一条更短的路——试试取陆路到海边去。

===Original===

At the same time, as I knew from experience, the difficulty of retaining guides, and as many circumstances might occur to prevent our progress in that direction, I declared my resolution not to attempt it, unless they would engage, if we could not after all proceed over land, to return with me, and continue our voyage to the discharge of the waters, whatever the distance might be. At all events, I declared, in the most solemn manner, that I would not abandon my design of reaching the sea, if I made the attempt alone, and that I did not despair of returning in safety to my friends.
This proposition met with the most zealous return, and they unanimously assured me, that they were as willing now as they had ever been, to abide by my resolutions, whatever they might be, and to follow me wherever I should go. I therefore requested them to prepare for an immediate departure, and at the same time gave notice to the man who had engaged to be our guide, to be in readiness to accompany us. When our determination to return up the river was made known, several of the natives took a very abrupt departure; but to those who remained, I gave a few useful articles, explaining to them at the same time, the advantages that would result to them, if their relations conducted me to the sea, along such a road as they had described. I had already given a moose skin to some of the women for the purpose of making shoes, which were now brought us; they were well sewed but ill-shaped, and a few beads were considered as a sufficient remuneration for the skill employed on them, Mr. Mackay, by my desire, engraved my name, and the date of the year on a tree.
When we were ready to depart, our guide proposed, for the sake of expedition, to go over land to his lodge, that he might get there before us, to make some necessary preparation for his journey. I did not altogether relish his design, but was obliged to consent: I thought it prudent, however, to send Mr. Mackay, and the two Indians along with him. Our place of rendezvous, was the subterraneous house which we passed yesterday.
At ten in the morning we embarked, and went up the current much faster than I expected with such a crazy vessel as that which carried us. We met our people at the house as had been appointed; but the Indian still continued to prefer going on by land, and it would have been needless for me to oppose him.

===Chinese===

同时，由于我凭经验深知留住向导之难，而许多情况又可能阻碍我们朝那个方向前进，我便声明：倘若陆路终究走不通，他们须答应同我一起回来，继续航行到这条水系的出海口，无论路程多远。无论如何，我以最郑重的方式宣告：纵然只身一人，我也不放弃到达海边的目标，而且我不怀疑自己能平安回到朋友们那里。
这一提议得到了最热忱的回报，他们异口同声地向我保证：他们一如既往，甘愿遵从我的任何决定，我到哪里，他们跟到哪里。我于是请他们即刻整装待发，同时通知那位应允做向导的人准备随行。我们溯河返回的决定一传开，几个土著便仓促离去；对留下的人，我送了几件有用的东西，并向他们说明：倘若他们的亲友照他们描述的那条路把我送到海边，将给他们带来多大的好处。我先前已把一张驼鹿皮给了几个女人做鞋，鞋此刻送来了：针脚细密，式样却笨拙，几颗珠子便算是对这番手艺足够的酬报。麦凯先生奉我之嘱，在一棵树上刻下了我的姓名和年份。
临出发时，我们的向导为了赶时间，提议取陆路先回他的棚屋，好赶在我们之前到家，为上路做些必要的准备。我并不喜欢他这个打算，却不得不应允；不过我想稳妥为上，便让麦凯先生和那两个印第安人同他一道走。我们会合的地点，定在我们昨天经过的那座地窟式屋子。
上午十点我们登船，逆流而上，我们这条破船走得比我想象的快得多。我们如约在那些棚屋同我的人会合；但那印第安人还是执意走陆路，我再拦他也是徒劳。

===Original===

He proceeded, therefore, with his former companions, whom I desired to keep him in good humour by every reasonable gratification. They were also furnished with a few articles that might be of use if they should meet strangers.
In a short time after we had left the house, I saw a wooden canoe coming down the river, with three natives in it, who, as soon as they perceived us, made for the shore, and hurried into the woods. On passing their vessel, we discovered it to be one of those which we had seen at the lodges. A severe gust of wind, with rain, came from the south-southeast. This we found to be a very prevalent wind in these parts. We soon passed another wooden canoe drawn stern foremost on the shore; a circumstance which we had not hitherto observed. The men worked very hard, and though I imagined we went ahead very fast, we could not reach the lodges, but landed for the night at nine, close to the encampment of two families of the natives whom we had formerly seen at the lodges. I immediately went and sat down with them, when they gave some roasted fish; two of my men who followed me were gratified also with some of their provisions. The youngest of the two natives now quitted the shed, and did not return during the time I remained there. I endeavoured to explain to the other by signs, the cause of my sudden return, which he appeared to understand. In the meantime my tent was pitched, and on my going to it, I was rather surprised that he did not follow me, as he had been constantly with me during the day and night I had passed with his party on going down. We, however, went to rest in a state of perfect security; nor had we the least apprehension for the safety of our people who were gone by land.
We were in our canoe by four this morning, and passed by the Indian hut, which appeared in a state of perfect tranquillity. We soon came in sight of the point where we first saw the natives, and at eight were much surprised and disappointed at seeing Mr. Mackay, and our two Indians coming alone from the ruins of a house that had been partly carried away by the ice and water, at a short distance below the place where we had appointed to meet. Nor was our surprise and apprehension diminished by the alarm which was painted in their countenances.

===Chinese===

他于是同原先的同伴们继续赶路，我嘱咐那些人，凡合理的要求尽量满足，让他保持好心情。他们还带了几件物品，万一遇上生人也许用得着。
我们离开那座屋子后不久，我看见一只木制独木舟顺流而下，舟中坐着三个土人，他们一发现我们，便直奔岸边，慌忙钻进林中。经过他们那条船时，我们认出它正是我们在棚屋那边见过的一只。一阵挟雨的狂风从南南东刮来。我们发现这是此地非常盛行的风。我们很快又驶过一只木制独木舟，船尾朝前拖在岸上；这种情况我们此前还从未见过。人们干得非常卖力，虽然我自以为我们前进得很快，却终究没能赶到那些棚屋，九点只得登岸过夜，紧挨着两户土人的营地——他们是我们先前在棚屋那里见过的。我立即过去同他们坐在一起，他们给了我一些烤鱼；跟着我来的两个人也分到了他们的一些食物。两个土人中较年轻的那个随即离开了棚子，我待在那里的整段时间都没有回来。我设法用手势向另一个说明我骤然折返的原因，他似乎明白了。这期间我的帐篷支好了，待我走进帐篷时，见他并没有跟来，不免有些诧异——我同他那一伙相处的那个白天和夜里，他可是一直跟在我身边的。不过我们还是安然入睡了；对取陆路而去的同伴们的安全，我们也毫不担心。
清晨四点我们便上了独木舟，经过那座印第安棚屋时，它显得一片安静。我们很快望见最初见到土人的那个地角，八点，却吃惊而失望地看见麦凯先生和我们的两个印第安人，从一座被冰和水冲走了半边的屋子废墟中独自走来——那屋子在我们约定会合处下方不远的地方。他们脸上惶惶的惊色，更添了我们的诧异与忧虑。

===Original===

When we had landed, they informed me that they had taken refuge in that place, with the determination to sell their lives, which they considered in the most imminent danger, as dear as possible. In a very short time after they had left us, they met a party of the Indians, whom we had known at this place, and were probably those whom we had seen to land from their canoe. They appeared to be in a state of extreme rage, and had their bows bent, with their arrows across them. The guide stopped to ask them some questions, which my people did not understand, and then set off with his utmost speed. Mr. Mackay, however, did not leave him till they were both exhausted with running. When the young man came up, he then said, that some treacherous design was meditated against them, as he was induced to believe from the declaration of the natives, who told him that they were going to do mischief, but refused to name the enemy. The guide then conducted them through very bad ways, as fast as they could run; and when he was desired to slacken his pace, he answered that they might follow him in any manner they pleased, but that he was impatient to get to his family, in order to prepare shoes, and other necessaries, for his journey. They did not, however, think it prudent to quit him, and he would not stop till ten at night. On passing a track that was but lately made, they began to be seriously alarmed, and on inquiring of the guide where they were, he pretended not to understand them. They then all laid down, exhausted with fatigue, and without any kind of covering: they were cold, wet, and hungry, but dared not light a fire, from the apprehension of an enemy. This comfortless spot they left at the dawn of the day, and, on their arrival at the lodges, found them deserted; the property of the Indians being scattered about, as if abandoned forever. The guide then made two or three trips into the woods, calling aloud, and bellowing like a madman. At length he set off in the same direction as they came, and had not since appeared. To heighten their misery, as they did not find us at the place appointed, they concluded that we were all destroyed, and had already formed their plan to take to the woods, and cross in as direct a line as they could proceed, to the waters of the Peace River, a scheme which could only be suggested by despair.

===Chinese===

登岸之后，他们告诉我，他们躲进那地方，打定主意要把性命卖得尽量贵——他们认定性命已危在旦夕。原来他们离开我们后不久，便遇上一伙我们在此地认识的印第安人，多半就是我们见到的从独木舟上登岸的那批。那伙人怒气冲天，弓已拉满，箭横在弦上。向导停下来问了他们几句——我的同伴们听不懂——随即撒腿飞奔。麦凯先生一路跟着他，直到两人都跑得筋疲力尽。等那年轻人赶上来，才说这些人正在酝酿什么奸计害他们——这是他从那些土人的话里推断的，那些人对他说他们要去作恶，却不肯说出敌人是谁。向导随后领着他们专拣坏路走，能跑多快跑多快；要他放慢脚步，他回答说，随你们爱怎么跟就怎么跟，他急着赶到家，为上路张罗鞋子和别的必需品。他们觉得离开他并不妥，他便这样一口气走到晚上十点才停。在经过一条新近踩出的径迹时，他们开始真正惊慌起来，问向导到了什么地方，他装作听不懂。于是众人躺倒在地，疲惫不堪，身上没有任何遮盖：又冷、又湿、又饿，却怕被敌人发现，不敢生火。天一亮他们便离开了那个凄凉的所在，到得那些棚屋时，发现已人去屋空；印第安人的东西四散丢着，仿佛永远不要了。向导随后钻进林子跑了两三趟，高声喊叫，像疯子一样吼。最后他顺原路来的方向去了，再没有露面。更添他们苦楚的是，在约定地点没有找到我们，他们断定我们已全部罹难，甚至已经拟好计划：钻进树林，取尽可能直的路线，穿到和平河（Peace River）的水系去——这方案唯有绝望才能想得出。

===Original===

They intended to have waited for us till noon, and if we did not appear by that time, to have entered without further delay on their desperate expedition.
This alarm among the natives was a very unexpected as well as perilous event, and my powers of conjecture were exhausted in searching for the cause of it. A general panic seized all around me, and any further prosecution of the voyage was now considered by them as altogether hopeless and impracticable. But without paying the least attention to their opinions or surmises, I ordered them to take everything out of the canoe, except six packages: when that was done, I left four men to take care of the lading, and returned with the others to our camp of last night, where I hoped to find the two men, with their families, whom we had seen there, and to be able to bring them to lodge with us, when I should wait the issue of this mysterious business. This project, however, was disappointed, for these people had quitted their sheds in the silence of the night, and had not taken a single article of their little property with them.
These perplexing circumstances made a deep impression on my mind, not as to our immediate safety, for I entertained not the least apprehension of the Indians I had hitherto seen, even if their whole force should have been combined to attack us, but these untoward events seemed to threaten the prosecution of my journey; and I could not reflect on the possibility of such a disappointment but with sensations little short of agony. Whatever might have been the wavering disposition of the people on former occasions, they were now decided in their opinions as to the necessity of returning without delay; and when we came back to them, their cry was﻿—“Let us re-embark, and be gone.” This, however, was not my design, and in a more peremptory tone than I usually employed, they were ordered to unload the canoe, and take her out of the water. On examining our property, several articles appeared to be missing, which the Indians must have purloined; and among them were an axe, two knives, and the young men’s bag of medicines. We now took a position that was the best calculated for defence, got our arms in complete order, filled each man’s flask of powder, and distributed an hundred bullets, which were all that remained, while some were employed in melting down shot to make more.

===Chinese===

他们本打算等到正午，若我们还不露面，便毫不迟延地踏上那趟亡命的远征。
土人中的这场惊乱，既出乎意料又充满凶险，我穷尽了猜测也找不出原因。四周一片普遍的恐慌，在他们看来，此行任何进一步的推进都已全然无望、全然不可行。但我全然不理会他们的意见和猜测，命人把独木舟里的一切都搬出来，只留六件包裹；搬完之后，我留下四个人看守货载，带着其余的人返回昨晚的营地，指望在那里找到那两户带着家眷、我们见过的人，把他们带到我们这边同住，静待这桩离奇事情的结局。然而这一打算又落了空：那些人已在寂静的夜里弃棚而去，连自己那一点点家当都没带走一件。
这些扑朔迷离的情况在我心上刻下了深深的印痕——倒不是为我们眼下的安全担忧，因为对迄今所见的这些印第安人，即便他们合全力来攻，我也毫不畏惧；可这些逆料不到的变故似乎威胁着我的行程；一想到此行可能就此断送，我便痛楚得几乎难以自持。无论我的人从前如何摇摆，此刻他们已拿定主意，认定必须毫不耽搁地返回；我们回到他们那里时，他们的呼声是——“上船，走吧。”然而这不是我的打算，我以比平常更严厉的口吻，命他们卸空独木舟，把它抬出水。清点物品时，发现少了几件，定是印第安人顺手摸走的，其中包括一把斧头、两把刀，还有年轻人们的那袋药品。我们随即抢占了一处最利于防守的位置，把武器一一整肃停当，给每人火药壶装满药，分发了一百颗弹丸——这已是全部存余——同时有些人忙着熔霞弹赶制新的。

===Original===

The weather was so cloudy, that I had not an opportunity of taking an observation.
While we were employed in making these preparations, we saw an Indian in a canoe come down the river, and land at the huts, which he began to examine. On perceiving us he stood still, as if in a state of suspense, when I instantly dispatched one of my Indians towards him, but no persuasions could induce him to have confidence in us; he even threatened that he would hasten to join his friends, who would come and kill us. At the conclusion of this menace he disappeared. On the return of my young man, with this account of the interview, I pretended to discredit the whole, and attributed it to his own apprehensions and alarms. This, however, he denied, and asked with a look and tone of resentment, whether he had ever told me a lie? Though he was but a young man, he said, he had been on war excursions before he came with me, and that he should no longer consider me as a wise man, which he had hitherto done.
To add to our distresses we had not an ounce of gum for the reparation of the canoe, and not one of the men had sufficient courage to venture into the woods to collect it. In this perplexing situation I entertained the hope that in the course of the night some of the natives would return, to take away a part at least of the things which they had left behind them, as they had gone away without the covering necessary to defend them from the weather and the flies. I therefore ordered the canoe to be loaded, and dropped to an old house, one side of which, with its roof, had been carried away by the water; but the three remaining angles were sufficient to shelter us from the woods. I then ordered two strong piquets to be driven into the ground, to which the canoe was fastened, so that if we were hard pressed we had only to step on board and push off. We were under the necessity of making a smoke to keep off the swarms of flies, which would have otherwise tormented us; but we did not venture to excite a blaze, as it would have been a mark for the arrows of the enemy. Mr. Mackay and myself, with three men kept alternate watch, and allowed the Indians to do as they fancied. I took the first watch, and the others laid down in their clothes by us. I also placed a sentinel at a small distance, who was relieved every hour. The weather was cloudy, with showers of rain.
*Tuesday, June 25.

===Chinese===

天气阴沉，我没有机会进行观测。
我们正忙着这些准备时，看见一个印第安人乘独木舟顺流而下，在那些棚屋处登岸，动手翻检。他一发现我们便僵在那里，像是进退两难，我当即派出我的一名印第安人朝他走去，但无论怎样劝说都不能使他信任我们；他甚至威胁说，要赶紧去找他的同伙，来把我们杀掉。撜下这句威胁，他便不见了。我的年轻人带着这次交涉的消息回来后，我假装全然不信，把这归因于他自己的疑惧和恐慌。他不服，带着愤愤的神色和口气问我：他可曾对我说过谎？他说，别看他年轻，跟我来之前也出征过，往后他再不能像从前那样把我当作智者了。
更添我们困窘的是，我们连一盎司修补独木舟的树胶都没有了，而人们中没有一个有胆量钻进林子去采。在这为难的局面里，我仍怀着一线希望：夜里总会有土人回来，把他们留下的东西至少取走一部分——他们走时连遮风避雨、抵御蚊虫的东西都没有带。于是我命人装上独木舟，移到一座旧屋子那边；屋子的一侧连同屋顶已被水冲走，剩下的三个角也足以替我们挡住林子那面的来路。我又命人在地上打进两根粗壮的木桩，把独木舟拴在上面，这样一旦被逼急了，我们只消跳上船撜开便是。我们不得不燃起烟堆驱赶成群的飞虫，不然它们定会折磨死我们；但我们不敢让火燃出明焰，免得成为敌人箭矢的靶子。麦凯先生和我带着三个人轮班守望，任凭那些印第安人各行其是。我带了头一班，其余的人和衣躺在我们旁边。我还在不远处布了一个哨，每小时换一次岗。天气多云，时有阵雨。
*6月25日，星期二

===Original===

*﻿—At one I called up the other watch, and laid down to a small portion of broken rest. At five I arose, and as the situation which we left yesterday was preferable to that which we then occupied, I determined to return to it. On our arrival Mr. Mackay informed me that the men had expressed their dissatisfaction to him in a very unreserved manner, and had in very strong terms declared their resolution to follow me no further in my proposed enterprise. I did not appear, however, to have received such communications from him, and continued to employ my whole thoughts in contriving means to bring about a reconciliation with the natives, which alone would enable me to procure guides, without whose assistance it would be impossible for me to proceed, when my darling project would end in disappointment.
At twelve we saw a man coming with the stream upon a raft, and he must have discovered us before we perceived him, as he was working very hard to get to the opposite shore, where he soon landed, and instantly fled into the woods. I now had a meridional altitude, which gave 60° 23′ natural horizon (the angle being more than the sextant could measure with the artificial horizon) one mile and a half distant; and the eye five feet above the level of the water, gave 52° 47′ 51″ north latitude.
While I was thus employed, the men loaded the canoe, without having received any orders from me, and as this was the first time they had ventured to act in such a decided manner, I naturally concluded that they had preconcerted a plan for their return. I thought it prudent, however, to take no notice of this transaction, and to wait the issue of future circumstances. At this moment our Indians perceived a person in the edge of the woods above us, and they were immediately dispatched to discover who it was. After a short absence they returned with a young woman whom we had seen before: her language was not clearly comprehended by us, so that we could not learn from her, at least with any degree of certainty, the cause of this unfortunate alarm that had taken place among the natives. She told us that her errand was to fetch some things which she had left behind her; and one of the dogs whom we found here, appeared to acknowledge her as his mistress. We treated her with great kindness, gave her something to eat, and added a present of such articles as we thought might please her.

===Chinese===

*——一点钟，我叫起另一班守望的人，自己躺下打个施。五点起身，由于我们昨天离开的位置比眼下占的这处强，我决定搬回去。到达之后，麦凯先生告诉我，人们已向他非常放直地吐露了不满，并用非常强硬的话宣告：对我拟议的事业，他们绝不再跟进一步。我却装作没有从他那里听到这些话，一心只想着设法同土人重修旧好——唯有如此我才能弄到向导，没有向导我便寸步难行，我的心爱的计划也将以失望告终。
十二点，我们看见一个人乘着木筏顺流而下；他必定在我们发现他之前就望见了我们，只见他拼命往对岸划，很快上了岸，立刻逃进林中。这时我测了一个子午高度，用天然地平线（角度超过了六分仪配人工地平线所能测的范围）得60°23′，地平线在一英里半外；眼高五英尺于水面之上，折得北纬52°47′51″。
我正忙着，人们没等我的命令便给独木舟装了载；这是他们头一回胆敢如此独断行事，我自然断定他们已预谋好一个返回的方案。但我想还是不动声色为妥，静观事态的发展。就在这时，我们的印第安人发现上方林缘里有个人影，他们立即被派去查明是谁。去了不久，他们带回一个我们见过的年轻女人：她的话我们听不大懂，因此至少无法确切弄清这场不幸的、在土人中间爆发的惊乱究竟因何而起。她说她此来是取几件落下的东西；我们在这里见到的一条狗，似乎认她是主人。我们待她极为和善，给她东西吃，还添送了几样我们想会讨她喜欢的物品。

===Original===

On her expressing a wish to leave us, we readily consented to her departure, and indulged the hope that her reception would induce the natives to return in peace, and give us an opportunity to convince them, that we had no hostile designs whatever against them. On leaving us, she went up the river, without taking a single article of her own, and the dog followed. The wind was changeable throughout the day, and there were several showers in the course of it.
Though a very apparent anxiety prevailed among the people for their departure, I appeared to be wholly inattentive to it, and at eight in the evening I ordered four men to step into the canoe, which had been loaded for several hours, and drop down to our guardhouse, and my command was immediately obeyed: the rest of us proceeded there by land. When I was yet at a considerable distance from the house, and thought it impossible for an arrow to reach it, having a bow and quiver in my hand, I very imprudently let fly an arrow, when, to my astonishment and infinite alarm, I heard it strike a log of the house. The men who had just landed, imagined that they were attacked by an enemy from the woods. Their confusion was in proportion to their imaginary danger, and on my arrival I found that the arrow had passed within a foot of one of the men; though it had no point, the weapon, incredible as it may appear, had entered an hard, dry log of wood upwards of an inch. But this was not all: for the men readily availed themselves of this circumstance, to remark upon the danger of remaining in the power of a people possessed of such means of destruction. Mr. Mackay having the first watch, I laid myself down in my cloak.
*Wednesday, June 26.*﻿—At midnight a rustling noise was heard in the woods which created a general alarm, and I was awakened to be informed of the circumstance, but heard nothing. At one I took my turn of the watch, and our dog continued unceasingly to run backwards and forwards along the skirts of the wood in a state of restless vigilance. At two in the morning the sentinel informed me, that he saw something like an human figure creeping along on all-fours about fifty paces above us.

===Chinese===

她表示想走，我们欣然应允，并怀着这样的希望：她受到的接待会促使土人平安归来，给我们一个机会向他们证明，我们对他们绝无敌意。她离开我们时溯河而上，自己的一件东西也没拿，那条狗跟着去了。全天风向多变，途中下了几阵雨。
尽管人们归心似箭、焦急形于颜色，我却装作全不理会；晚上八点，我命四个人跨进那条早已装好几个小时的独木舟，顺水放到我们的岗屋去，命令立刻执行了；其余的人取道陆路前往。当我离那屋子还很远、心想箭绝无可能射到时，手里正拿着弓和箭囊，便非常轻率地放了一箭；使我骇异、令我惊惶不已的是，竟听见它射中了屋子的一根木料。刚上岸的人们还以为遭到了林中敌人的袭击。他们那份慌乱正同他们想象中的危险相称；待我赶到，才发现那支箭贴着一个人的身边不到一英尺飞过；虽然箭无镞，那武器——说来令人难以置信——竟深深扎进一根又硬又干的木头一英寸有余。这还不算：人们立刻抓住这件事，议论落在这样一个拥有如此杀人利器的民族的势力范围里有多危险。麦凯先生带了头一班守望，我裹着斗篷躺下了。
*6月26日，星期三*——午夜，林中传来一阵窸窣声，引起一片惊慌，我被唤醒并得知这一情况，却什么也没听见。一点我接班守望，我们的狗不停地在林缘来回奔跑，警觉得不肯安歇。凌晨两点，哨兵告诉我，他看见在我们上方约五十步的地方，有个像人形的东西在匍匐爬行。

===Original===

After some time had passed in our search, I at length discovered that his information was true, and it appeared to me that a bear had occasioned the alarm; but when day appeared, it proved to be an old, grey-haired, blind man, who had been compelled to leave his hiding-place by extreme hunger, being too infirm to join in the flight of the natives to whom he belonged. When I put my hand on this object of decaying nature, his alarm was so great, that I expected it would have thrown him into convulsions. I immediately led him to our fire which had been just lighted, and gave him something to eat, which he much wanted, as he had not tasted food for two days. When his hunger was satisfied, and he had got warm and composed, I requested him to acquaint me with the cause of that alarm which had taken place respecting us among his relations and friends, whose regard we appeared to have conciliated but a few days past. He replied, that very soon after we had left them, some natives arrived from above, who informed them that we were enemies; and our unexpected return, in direct contradiction to our own declarations, confirmed them in that opinion. They were now, he said, so scattered, that a considerable time would elapse, before they could meet again. We gave him the real history of our return, as well as of the desertion of our guide, and, at the same time, stated the impossibility of our proceeding, unless we procured a native to conduct us. He replied, that if he had not lost his sight, he would with the greatest readiness have accompanied us on our journey. He also confirmed the accounts which we had received of the country, and the route to the westward. I did not neglect to employ every argument in my power, that he might be persuaded of our friendly dispositions to the inhabitants wheresoever we might meet them.
At sunrise we perceived a canoe with one man in it on the opposite side of the river, and at our request, the blind man called to him to come to us, but he returned no answer, and continued his course as fast as he could paddle down the current. He was considered as a spy by my men, and I was confirmed in that opinion, when I saw a wooden canoe drifting with the stream close in to the other shore, where it was more than probable that some of the natives might be concealed.

===Chinese===

我们搜寻了一阵之后，我终于证实他所说属实；依我看，引起惊慌的像是一头熊；可天亮之后才发现，竟是一个灰发苍苍的瞎老头，他实在饿极了，才不得不离开藏身之处——他年迈体衰，跟不上同族人的逃亡。我把手放到这具衰朽的身躯上时，他的惊恐之状，直叫我担心会把他吓出抽风来。我立刻把他领到刚生起的火边，给了他一些吃的——他实在急需，已经两天没进食了。等他填饱了肚子、暖和镇定下来，我请他告诉我：几天前我们看似已博得他们的亲友好感，何以骤然对我们生出这场惊乱。他回答说，我们离开后不久，上游来了些土人，说我们是敌人；而我们出乎他们意料的折返，同我们自己先前的宣言截然相反，更坐实了这种说法。他说，如今他们散得到处都是，要重新聚齐，还须相当时日。我们把折返的实情，连同向导出逃的事，都对他们说了，同时说明：不弄到一个土人引路，我们便寸步难行。他答道，若不是双目失明，他定会万分乐意陪我们走这一程。他还证实了我们先前听到的关于这一带地方和西行路线的种种说法。我不忘使出浑身解数劝说，好让他确信，我们无论在哪里遇到这里的居民，都怀着友好的心意。
日出时分，我们看见河对岸有一只独木舟，里面坐着一个人；应我们的请求，瞎老头朝他喊话，叫他到我们这里来，但他一声不应，只顾拼命划着水流尽快往下游去了。我的人把他当成了奸细；待我看见一只木制独木舟贴着对岸顺流漂下——那里十有八九藏着土人——我也信了这一判断。

===Original===

It might, therefore, have been an useless enterprise, or perhaps fatal to the future success of our undertaking, if we had pursued these people, as they might, through fear have employed their arms against us, and provoked us to retaliate.
The old man informed me, that some of the natives whom I had seen here were gone up the river, and those whom I saw below had left their late station to gather a root in the plains, which, when dried, forms a considerable article in their winter stock of provisions. He had a woman, he said, with him, who used to see us walking along the small adjoining river, but when he called her he received no answer, so that she had probably fled to join her people. He informed me, also, that he expected a considerable number of his tribe to come on the upper part of the river to catch fish for their present support, and to cure them for their winter store; among whom he had a son and two brothers.
